Functional Specification - JLPT N5 Grammar Tutor & Test
(GitHub-Hosted Static Web App)  •  Version 4  •  Amended 2026-04-30
Target learner: late-N5 (wrapping up N5; preparing for JLPT N5 exam).
Live site: https://gauravaccentureproducts.github.io/jlpt-n5-tutor/
Predecessors: v1 (KnowledgeBank/sources.md era) and v2 (post-Brief-1) preserved as separate files.

[Heading 1] 0) Changelog from v2
Shipped between 2026-04-29 and 2026-04-30 across UX Brief 2 (4 phases), the Pass-9 content correction, the Pass-10 audio fixes, and a battery of UI follow-ups.
[Heading 2] New routes & flow (Brief 2 §1, §14)
[List Bullet] `#/home` is now the default route. First-time visitors see a CTA + 3 pillar cards; returning visitors see a Continue card + Today's review queue + 7-day streak heatmap.
[List Bullet] `#/learn` is now a 5-card hub (Reference: Grammar / Vocabulary / Kanji ; Practice: Dokkai / Listening). 3+2 semantic split avoids orphaning.
[List Bullet] `#/learn/grammar` - 187 grammar cards in 32 categories with a sticky chip-rail jump menu.
[List Bullet] `#/learn/vocab` - 1002 vocab cards in 40 sections (only first section open by default) with a 40-chip jump menu that opens + scrolls the target section.
[List Bullet] `#/learn/vocab/<form>` - per-word detail page (form / reading / gloss / 5 example sentences sourced from grammar.json by either kanji form OR kana reading).
[List Bullet] `#/kanji` - card grid (106 entries with glyph + meaning + first kun/on); `#/kanji/<glyph>` - per-kanji detail page with prev/next nav.
[List Bullet] `#/test/<n>` for n in {20,30,50} starts a test of that length directly.
[Heading 2] Settings panel additions (Brief 2 §5)
[List Bullet] 3-mode furigana (Always show / Hide on known kanji / Never) replaces the binary toggle. Live preview next to the radios.
[List Bullet] Per-kanji 'I know this' flag - click any kanji glyph anywhere to open a popover with on/kun/meaning + a checkbox; flag persists in `localStorage.knownKanji` and feeds the 'hide-known' furigana mode.
[List Bullet] Audio playback speed (0.75x / 1.0x / 1.25x) - applied via MutationObserver to every `<audio>` element on every route render.
[List Bullet] Reduce motion override (auto / on / off) - sets `data-reduce-motion` on `<html>`; CSS overrides animation/transition durations.
[List Bullet] Typed-phrase reset ('Type RESET to confirm') replaces the legacy double-confirm dialog.
[List Bullet] Export schema bumped 1 → 2 to include the new `knownKanji` and `streak` keys (settings, history, results, knownKanji, streak). v1 imports remain accepted - missing keys default to empty.
[Heading 2] PWA / offline upgrades (Brief 2 §12)
[List Bullet] Service worker `jlpt-n5-tutor-v24` - stale-while-revalidate for the shell (HTML/CSS/JS) and cache-first for content (data/audio/locales).
[List Bullet] 'Update available' toast - SW posts a SW_UPDATE_AVAILABLE message when new shell bytes are detected; click Reload to skipWaiting + reload.
[List Bullet] PWA install banner - one-time, dismissible, persists in localStorage.
[List Bullet] Offline indicator - chip in top-right that toggles with `navigator.onLine`. Hidden when online.
[Heading 2] Mobile / responsive / a11y (Brief 2 §9, §10)
[List Bullet] Bottom nav at ≤480 px - primary nav becomes a fixed bar with `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` honored.
[List Bullet] 44 px tap-target floor enforced via CSS on every interactive element.
[List Bullet] Skeleton screens replace the legacy 'Loading...' text on every route. 5-second timeout shows a real 'Couldn't load - Retry' UI.
[List Bullet] Empty states with routing buttons for Review / Summary / Test (no completed tests / no progress yet / no due items).
[List Bullet] Print stylesheet (`@media print`) hides chrome, expands `<details>`, switches to serif. Clean Learn-lesson printables.
[Heading 2] Power user / search / shortcuts (Brief 2 §7, §8)
[List Bullet] Cross-corpus search in the secondary nav: indexes grammar (id/pattern/meaning/explanation), vocab (form/reading/gloss, capped at 1500), kanji (glyph/on/kun/meanings). Results group by type with counts. `/` keyboard shortcut focuses the input.
[List Bullet] Global keyboard shortcuts (`js/shortcuts.js`): 1-4 picks Nth choice; Space reveal/flip; Enter clicks primary/Submit; ? opens cheatsheet overlay; Esc dismisses overlays.
[List Bullet] Quit-prompt in Test - `__testInProgress` flag set on test start; beforeunload + hashchange interceptors confirm 'Quit this test?' and revert the hash on cancel.
[Heading 2] Audio + content corrections (Pass 9, Pass 10)
[List Bullet] 491 MP3 files - 449 grammar examples + 30 reading passages + 12 listening scripts (~19 MB) generated via gTTS at build time. App degrades gracefully when MP3s are absent.
[List Bullet] `tools/build_audio.py:normalize_for_tts()` - converts ASCII digits to kanji digits before passing to gTTS so '3さつ' reads as 'さんさつ' and not as 'スリーさつ'. 274 occurrences across the corpus normalized.
[List Bullet] `data/n5_kanji_readings.json` - 35 wrong-primary readings fixed (本 もと→ほん, 時 とき→じ, 月 つき→がつ, 学 まな→がく, 人 ひと→にん, etc.) so auto-furigana renders the N5-context-most-common reading.
[List Bullet] Pass 9 (external content brief) - 27 + 4 sweeps + 7 cross-file consistency checks raised, all 38 fixed, 0 open across moji/goi/bunpou/dokkai/authentic question files.
[List Bullet] Pass 10 (audio + auto-furigana) - 309 findings (274 ASCII-digit + 35 wrong-primary), all closed.

[List Bullet] Pass 1-9 content corrections: 153 raised, 153 closed.
[List Bullet] Pass 10 audio + auto-furigana: 309 raised, 309 closed.
[List Bullet] Total: 462 raised, 462 closed, 0 open across 10 audit passes spanning JLPT paper-maker, native-teacher, external 日本語教師, and TTS / rendering correctness perspectives.

[Heading 1] 0.1) Changelog from v1 (historical)
[List Bullet] §4.2 Furigana default flipped to OFF on N5 kanji - matches a late-N5 learner. Per-session toggle to flip ON when desired.
[List Bullet] §4.3 Lesson template expanded from 5 blocks to 7 - adds Form & Connection Rules and Common Mistakes / Contrasts blocks.
[List Bullet] §4.4 NEW Canonical pattern source - locked to local file `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md` (23 categories, exhaustive), not an external website.
[List Bullet] §4.5 NEW meaning_ja rule - N5 kanji allowed; no out-of-scope kanji or vocab; no romaji anywhere.
[List Bullet] §4.6 NEW Example variety rule - 4-6 examples for fundamentals with mandatory form variety (`affirmative` / `negative` / `question` / `past` / `dialogue`).
[List Bullet] §4.7 NEW N5 vocabulary whitelist - content lint enforces scope using `KnowledgeBank/vocabulary_n5.md`.
[List Bullet] §4.8 NEW N5 kanji whitelist - furigana rule and content lint enforce scope using `KnowledgeBank/kanji_n5.md`.
[List Bullet] §5.3 Submit button rule clarified - visible-but-disabled with tooltip and remaining-count, not hidden.
[List Bullet] §5.7 NEW Diagnostic test - optional 10-question placement check on first run.
[List Bullet] §5.8 NEW Drill mode (SRS-light) - promoted from Phase 2 roadmap to Phase 1. Intervals 1d / 3d / 7d / 14d.
[List Bullet] §6.2 Test engine - adds `sentence_order` (並べ替え) question type.
[List Bullet] §6.5 NEW Per-distractor explanations for high-confusion questions.
[List Bullet] §6.6 NEW Threshold-based weak pattern detection - `errors/attempts ≥ 0.5 AND attempts ≥ 2` over rolling history, not single-test snapshot.
[List Bullet] §7.1 / §7.2 schemas extended - pattern adds `category`, `order`, `form_rules`, `common_mistakes`; question adds `direction`, `distractor_explanations`, `high_confusion`, `sentence_order` shape.
[List Bullet] §8 Tech stack locked - Vanilla HTML + CSS + JavaScript with ES modules. No build step. No npm. Open `index.html` to run.
[List Bullet] §9 Repo structure updated - co-locates app with existing content files in `/JLPT/N5/`.

[Heading 1] 1) Purpose & Goals
Build a PC-friendly, browser-based web app that:
[List Bullet] Teaches all JLPT N5 grammar patterns in a structured Chapter 1 format (TOC → meaning → form/connection → explanation → examples → common mistakes → simple Japanese meaning).
[List Bullet] Provides an auto-graded test (Chapter 2) attemptable on a PC.
[List Bullet] Enforces: Submit button visible-but-disabled until all questions are answered; results display immediately after Submit (no reload).
[List Bullet] Performs gap analysis using rolling-history thresholds (Chapter 3) and reteaches weak patterns with form rules, common mistakes, and fresh examples.
[List Bullet] Drives a Drill mode (Spaced Repetition) over weak patterns at 1d / 3d / 7d / 14d intervals.
[List Bullet] Summarizes mastered / weak / untested patterns and recommends next steps (Chapter 4).
[List Bullet] Runs on GitHub Pages as a static site with zero build step. Anyone with a modern browser can clone and double-click `index.html`.
[Heading 1] 2) Target Users & Personas
[List Bullet] Learner (Krivi): Wrapping up N5; preparing for the JLPT N5 exam. Already knows most N5 kanji visually. Default mode hides furigana on N5 kanji.
[List Bullet] Tutor / Admin (You): Maintains grammar content + question bank in source-of-truth files (`KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md`, `KnowledgeBank/vocabulary_n5.md`, `KnowledgeBank/kanji_n5.md`, `/data/grammar.json`, `/data/questions.json`). Reviews progress.
[Heading 1] 3) Scope
[Heading 2] In Scope (Phase 1)
[List Bullet] Grammar lessons (TOC + 7-block pattern detail pages).
[List Bullet] Auto-graded test engine (MCQ + dropdown fill + sentence ordering 並べ替え).
[List Bullet] Instant scoring with per-question review and per-distractor explanations.
[List Bullet] Auto-generated weak grammar pattern list using rolling threshold detection.
[List Bullet] Drill mode (SRS-light) over weak patterns.
[List Bullet] Diagnostic placement test on first run.
[List Bullet] Local progress tracking via LocalStorage (required, not optional).
[List Bullet] GitHub Pages deployment with no build step.
[List Bullet] Furigana toggle (per-session) for N5-kanji visibility.
[List Bullet] Content lint script (run by author): flags non-N5 vocab/kanji in `/data/*.json`.
[Heading 2] Out of Scope (Phase 1)
[List Bullet] User accounts / login.
[List Bullet] Server-side analytics.
[List Bullet] Open-ended writing or essay evaluation.
[List Bullet] Audio / listening practice.
[List Bullet] Adaptive testing with Anki-style ease factors (Drill is Leitner-light only).
[List Bullet] Multi-learner profiles.
[Heading 1] 4) Content Rules (Hard Requirements)
[Heading 2] 4.1 JLPT N5 Scope Constraints
[List Bullet] All lesson examples and test content must use only N5-scope vocabulary (per `KnowledgeBank/vocabulary_n5.md`) and N5-scope kanji (per `KnowledgeBank/kanji_n5.md`).
[List Bullet] If out-of-scope text is unavoidable: prefer N5 alternative; otherwise apply furigana rule (§4.2) to the specific word only.
[List Bullet] No romaji anywhere in lesson or test content. Force kana reading.
[Heading 2] 4.2 Furigana Rules (Strict)
[List Bullet] Default: furigana OFF on N5-scope kanji. This matches a late-N5 learner; the goal is reading practice, not crutches.
[List Bullet] Always furigana ON for any kanji or word beyond N5 scope, on the specific word only - never the entire sentence.
[List Bullet] Per-session toggle: "Show furigana on N5 kanji" - learner can flip ON for extra support; OFF is the default and persists in LocalStorage.
[List Bullet] Furigana rendering must be a true ruby annotation (`<ruby><rt>`), not parenthetical. Must support per-character or per-word annotation, not whole-sentence.
[Heading 2] 4.3 Lesson Template (Chapter 1) - 7 Blocks
For each grammar pattern, display in this exact order:
[List Number] Pattern name - e.g., 〜です／〜ます
[List Number] Meaning (English) - short gloss.
[List Number] Form & connection rules - what does the pattern attach to (noun / い-adj / な-adj / verb-which-form)? Include a small conjugation table where applicable (affirmative / negative / past / past-negative / question).
[List Number] Explanation & usage (English) - when to use it, register, nuance.
[List Number] Example sentences (4-6 for fundamentals; 2-3 for narrow patterns) - must follow the form-variety rule (§4.6).
[List Number] Common mistakes / contrasts - pairs like 「は vs が」, 「に vs で」, 「あります vs います」, 「上手 vs 得意」. At least one entry where applicable. Include a strikethrough wrong sentence with one-line "why this is wrong" where helpful.
[List Number] Meaning (Japanese, simple) - N5 kanji allowed, no out-of-scope words, hiragana otherwise. Provides reading reinforcement.
[Heading 2] 4.4 Canonical Pattern Source
[List Bullet] The canonical pattern coverage list is the local file `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md` (in this directory) - 23 categories, exhaustive at N5 level.
[List Bullet] TOC ordering follows that file's 23-category structure: copula → particles → demonstratives → question words → verbs (ます-form, plain form) → te-form → adjectives → existence → comparison → desiderative → counters → time → conjunctions → giving/receiving → causation → nominalization → set patterns → frequency → polite phrases → other core patterns → honorifics.
[List Bullet] Every pattern listed in `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md` MUST have a matching entry in `/data/grammar.json`. A coverage-check script (`tools/check_coverage.py`) enforces this - fails commit if a pattern is missing.
[List Bullet] Authoritative reference sources for content quality are documented in `KnowledgeBank/sources.md` (Genki, Minna no Nihongo, Try!, Makino & Tsutsui, Marugoto, Bunpro, etc.).
[Heading 2] 4.5 meaning_ja Rule
[List Bullet] N5 kanji ALLOWED for content nouns/verbs.
[List Bullet] All other words MUST be hiragana.
[List Bullet] No out-of-scope kanji or vocabulary.
[List Bullet] No romaji.
[List Bullet] If an out-of-scope word is unavoidable, apply furigana to that word only.
[Heading 2] 4.6 Example Variety Rule
For fundamental patterns (high frequency: は、が、を、に、〜ます、〜ました、〜てください、〜たい、etc.), provide 4-6 examples with form variety:
[List Bullet] At least 1 affirmative.
[List Bullet] At least 1 negative.
[List Bullet] At least 1 question.
[List Bullet] At least 1 past tense (where applicable).
[List Bullet] Optionally 1 mini-dialogue.
Each example MUST declare a `form` field: `affirmative` | `negative` | `question` | `past` | `dialogue`.
For narrow patterns (e.g., specific particles like 「や」), 2-3 examples are acceptable.
[Heading 2] 4.7 N5 Vocabulary Whitelist
[List Bullet] `KnowledgeBank/vocabulary_n5.md` is the canonical N5 vocabulary scope list.
[List Bullet] Generated artifact `/data/n5_vocab_whitelist.json` is consumed by the content lint script.
[List Bullet] Lint script flags any token in `/data/grammar.json` and `/data/questions.json` that is not on the whitelist (excluding hiragana-only function words / particles / explicit furigana-tagged out-of-scope words).
[Heading 2] 4.8 N5 Kanji Whitelist
[List Bullet] `KnowledgeBank/kanji_n5.md` is the canonical N5 kanji scope list.
[List Bullet] Generated artifact `/data/n5_kanji_whitelist.json` is consumed by the furigana renderer and the lint script.
[List Bullet] The furigana renderer treats any kanji NOT on this whitelist as out-of-scope and renders furigana for it; kanji ON this whitelist render furigana only when the user toggle is ON.
[Heading 1] 5) UX / UI Requirements
[Heading 2] 5.1 Global Navigation
Primary navigation tabs (in this order):
[List Bullet] Learn (Chapter 1)
[List Bullet] Test (Chapter 2)
[List Bullet] Drill (NEW - SRS-driven mini-sessions on weak patterns; shows count badge of items due today)
[List Bullet] Review Weak Areas (Chapter 3)
[List Bullet] Summary (Chapter 4)
[Heading 2] 5.2 Chapter 1 - Learn
[List Bullet] TOC page lists all N5 grammar patterns grouped by category (per `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md`'s 23 categories).
[List Bullet] Each TOC entry shows pattern name + a small "known / weak / untested" badge derived from rolling history.
[List Bullet] Clicking a pattern opens the 7-block detail view (§4.3).
[List Bullet] Provide Next / Previous pattern navigation within and across categories.
[List Bullet] Provide search/filter by keyword (pattern name, meaning, or category).
[List Bullet] Provide a "Mark as known" checkbox per pattern (manual override; updates rolling history).
[Heading 2] 5.3 Chapter 2 - Test
[Heading 3] Test Setup
[List Bullet] Default test length: 20 questions. Selectable: 20 / 30 / 50.
[List Bullet] First-time learner: a one-time prompt offers the Diagnostic Test (§5.7) before the first real test.
[List Bullet] Question types (auto-gradable):
[List Bullet]   Multiple choice (single correct).
[List Bullet]   Fill-in-blank with dropdown (preferred for particles).
[List Bullet]   Sentence ordering (並べ替え) - 4-5 word/phrase tiles, click to order.
[List Bullet]   Optional: strict short input (exact match with normalization rules).
[List Bullet] Question sampling MUST balance across categories (no single-category dominance), unless test length < 8.
[Heading 3] Attempting
[List Bullet] Progress indicator: "Question X of N" + a thin progress bar.
[List Bullet] Allow Next / Previous navigation.
[List Bullet] Optional "Flag question" feature (icon toggle; flagged items grouped at end of review).
[Heading 3] Submit Button Rule (Mandatory)
[List Bullet] Submit button is always visible on the last question.
[List Bullet] Until every question has an answer, Submit is disabled and shows a tooltip on hover: "Answer all questions to submit" plus a remaining-count: "3 questions still unanswered".
[List Bullet] Once all questions are answered, Submit becomes enabled (no extra confirmation step).
[List Bullet] This rule is non-negotiable across all question types and test lengths.
[Heading 3] Post-Submit Behavior (Mandatory)
[List Bullet] Clicking Submit triggers immediate in-place results rendering.
[List Bullet] No manual grading.
[List Bullet] No page reload.
[List Bullet] Test responses are appended to rolling history in LocalStorage before the results screen renders.
[Heading 2] 5.4 Results Screen (After Submit)
Three sections, in this order:
[Heading 3] (a) Score Summary
[List Bullet] Total score, e.g., 17/20.
[List Bullet] Correct count / incorrect count.
[List Bullet] Percentage.
[List Bullet] Time taken (optional).
[Heading 3] (b) Answer Review (per question)
[List Bullet] Question text.
[List Bullet] Learner's answer (highlighted; red if incorrect, green if correct).
[List Bullet] Correct answer (clearly shown).
[List Bullet] Explanation of why the correct answer is correct.
[List Bullet] Per-distractor explanation for high-confusion questions: only the distractor the learner picked is explained (not all four), to avoid cognitive overload.
[List Bullet] Tagged grammar pattern(s) with link to Chapter 1 detail page.
[Heading 3] (c) Grammar Gap List
[List Bullet] Patterns flagged as weak by rolling-history threshold (§6.6): `errors/attempts ≥ 0.5 AND attempts ≥ 2`.
[List Bullet] Each item links to Chapter 3 (Review Weak Areas).
[List Bullet] Each weak pattern is auto-queued into Drill at the 1-day interval.
[Heading 2] 5.5 Chapter 3 - Review Weak Areas
For each weak grammar pattern, display:
[List Bullet] Pattern name.
[List Bullet] Meaning + explanation (English).
[List Bullet] Form & connection rules + common mistakes (re-shown with extra emphasis).
[List Bullet] Why the wrong answer was wrong (per-distractor explanations pulled from missed questions).
[List Bullet] New examples (4-6 if fundamental, 2-3 otherwise) - must be DIFFERENT from Chapter 1 examples to avoid memorization-by-position.
[List Bullet] "Drill these now" button → opens an SRS Drill session focused on this pattern.
[Heading 2] 5.6 Chapter 4 - Summary
[List Bullet] Mastered patterns - seen ≥ 2 times AND error rate < 30% over rolling history (or manually marked known).
[List Bullet] Patterns needing more practice - `errors/attempts ≥ 0.5 AND attempts ≥ 2`.
[List Bullet] Untested patterns - seen 0-1 times → "Take a test to assess."
[List Bullet] Suggested next steps: Drill due items / Re-attempt test on weak patterns / Take Diagnostic if not yet done.
[List Bullet] Visual: a heatmap-style grid by category showing strength (green) / weak (red) / untested (gray).
[Heading 2] 5.7 NEW: Diagnostic Test (First Run)
[List Bullet] Prompt on first visit (and re-runnable from Settings): "Take a 10-question diagnostic to map your current strengths?"
[List Bullet] 10 questions, sampled across the 23 categories of `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md` with priority on the highest-frequency categories (copula, particles, verbs, adjectives, te-form, tai-form, counters, conjunctions, demonstratives, question words).
[List Bullet] Results don't count toward score history but DO seed the initial weak-pattern list and Drill queue.
[List Bullet] Skippable - learner can go straight to lessons or full test.
[Heading 2] 5.8 NEW: Drill Mode (SRS-light)
[List Bullet] Driven by LocalStorage: per-pattern SRS state.
[List Bullet] Intervals: 1 day / 3 days / 7 days / 14 days (Leitner-style boxes).
[List Bullet] A pattern enters Drill on:
[List Bullet]   First miss in a Test or Diagnostic.
[List Bullet]   Any wrong answer in a previous Drill session.
[List Bullet] A pattern advances to the next interval on a correct answer; resets to 1d on wrong.
[List Bullet] A pattern graduates (leaves Drill) after 4 consecutive correct answers across boxes.
[List Bullet] UI: "Drill" tab shows a count badge of patterns due today.
[List Bullet] A Drill session = 5-10 questions sampled from due patterns. Feedback is immediate per question (not batched like the full Test).
[List Bullet] Drill respects the visible-but-disabled Submit rule for any multi-question Drill batch ≥ 5 questions.
[Heading 1] 6) Functional Requirements (Detailed)
[Heading 2] 6.1 Grammar Content Management
[List Bullet] FR-L1: System shall load `/data/grammar.json` at startup.
[List Bullet] FR-L2: System shall render TOC grouped by category (per `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md`'s 23 categories), in declared order.
[List Bullet] FR-L3: System shall render pattern detail using the 7-block expanded template (§4.3).
[List Bullet] FR-L4: System shall support cross-linking from Test results → Chapter 1 pattern detail and Chapter 3 review.
[List Bullet] FR-L5: System shall enforce that every pattern in `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md` has a matching entry in `/data/grammar.json`. The `tools/check_coverage.py` script verifies this and exits non-zero if any pattern is missing.
[Heading 2] 6.2 Test Engine
[List Bullet] FR-T1: Generate tests from `/data/questions.json`. Each question requires: `id`, `grammarPatternId`, `type`, `correctAnswer`, `choices` (for MCQ/dropdown) OR `tiles`+`correctOrder` (for sentence_order). Recommended: `explanation_en`, `distractor_explanations`.
[List Bullet] FR-T2: Enforce "all answered before Submit becomes enabled" via visible-but-disabled rule (§5.3).
[List Bullet] FR-T3: Compute score instantly on Submit.
[List Bullet] FR-T4: Display results immediately after Submit (no reload).
[List Bullet] FR-T5: Produce gap list using rolling threshold (§6.6), not single-test snapshot.
[List Bullet] FR-T6: Support `sentence_order` question type with array-equality grading.
[List Bullet] FR-T7: Sample test questions to balance across categories - no single category may exceed `ceil(N / 5)` questions in a test of length N (where N ≥ 8).
[Heading 2] 6.3 Answer Normalization (if short input is used)
[List Bullet] FR-N1: Trim whitespace; convert full-width ↔ half-width as appropriate.
[List Bullet] FR-N2: Support multiple acceptable answers (array) where defined.
[List Bullet] FR-N3: Normalize hiragana / katakana variants where the question allows either.
[Heading 2] 6.4 Progress & Persistence (REQUIRED)
[List Bullet] FR-P1: Persist test results, weak patterns, SRS state, and rolling pattern history in LocalStorage.
[List Bullet] FR-P2: Provide a "Reset progress" button with a two-step confirmation.
[List Bullet] FR-P3: Persist learner's furigana toggle preference and last-selected test length.
[List Bullet] FR-P4: Use the LocalStorage key namespace `jlpt-n5-tutor:*` to avoid collisions.
[Heading 2] 6.5 NEW: Per-Distractor Explanations
[List Bullet] FR-D1: For questions with `high_confusion: true`, every distractor MUST have an explanation in `distractor_explanations`.
[List Bullet] FR-D2: Results screen shows the distractor explanation only for the choice the learner picked, not all distractors - to avoid information overload.
[List Bullet] FR-D3: Chapter 3 (Review Weak Areas) aggregates distractor explanations from all missed questions for the same pattern.
[Heading 2] 6.6 NEW: Weak Pattern Detection (Threshold-Based)
[List Bullet] FR-W1: A pattern is "weak" if `errors / attempts ≥ 0.5` AND `attempts ≥ 2` over rolling history (not single-test).
[List Bullet] FR-W2: Single-test results contribute to rolling history; rolling history is preserved across sessions in LocalStorage.
[List Bullet] FR-W3: A pattern graduates from "weak" to "mastered" after 4 consecutive correct answers across Drill or Test.
[List Bullet] FR-W4: Manual override: learner can mark a pattern "known" from Chapter 1, which sets it to mastered until the next miss.
[Heading 2] 6.7 NEW: SRS State Management
[List Bullet] FR-S1: Track per-pattern SRS box: `1d` / `3d` / `7d` / `14d` / `graduated`.
[List Bullet] FR-S2: Surface patterns in Drill where `nextDue ≤ now`.
[List Bullet] FR-S3: Do NOT preemptively drill patterns with no missed history.
[List Bullet] FR-S4: Wrong answer in Drill resets the pattern to box `1d` and `consecutiveCorrect = 0`.
[Heading 1] 7) Data Model (JSON-First)
[Heading 2] 7.1 Grammar Pattern Object (UPDATED)
{
  "id": "n5-001",
  "pattern": "〜です／〜ます",
  "category": "copula",
  "categoryOrder": 1,
  "patternOrder": 1,
  "meaning_en": "Polite copula / polite verb ending",
  "meaning_ja": "ていねいな いいかた",
  "form_rules": {
    "attaches_to": ["noun", "na_adjective", "verb_stem"],
    "conjugations": [
      { "form": "affirmative_present", "example": "がくせいです" },
      { "form": "negative_present",    "example": "がくせいじゃありません" },
      { "form": "affirmative_past",    "example": "がくせいでした" },
      { "form": "negative_past",       "example": "がくせいじゃありませんでした" }
    ]
  },
  "explanation_en": "Used in polite speech. です for nouns/adjectives; ます for verbs.",
  "examples": [
    { "ja": "わたしは がくせいです。",            "form": "affirmative", "furigana": [] },
    { "ja": "わたしは がくせいじゃありません。", "form": "negative",    "furigana": [] },
    { "ja": "あなたは がくせいですか。",          "form": "question",    "furigana": [] },
    { "ja": "きのうは やすみでした。",            "form": "past",        "furigana": [] }
  ],
  "common_mistakes": [
    {
      "wrong": "わたしは がくせいです じゃありません。",
      "right": "わたしは がくせいじゃありません。",
      "why":   "Don't combine です and じゃありません. Use one or the other for tense/polarity."
    }
  ],
  "contrasts": [
    { "with_pattern_id": "n5-XXX", "note": "..." }
  ],
  "notes": ""
}

[Heading 2] 7.2 Question Object (UPDATED)
MCQ shape:
{
  "id": "q-0001",
  "grammarPatternId": "n5-010",
  "type": "mcq",
  "direction": "j_to_e",
  "prompt_ja": "(  )に はいる いちばん いい ものを えらんでください。",
  "question_ja": "わたしは きのう ともだち(  )あいました。",
  "choices": ["を", "に", "で", "が"],
  "correctAnswer": "に",
  "explanation_en": "会う takes に to mark the person you meet.",
  "distractor_explanations": {
    "を": "を marks a direct object. 会う is intransitive - doesn't take を.",
    "で": "で marks location of action, not the person met.",
    "が": "が marks subject/new info, not the person met."
  },
  "high_confusion": true,
  "difficulty": 1
}

Sentence-order shape:
{
  "id": "q-0050",
  "grammarPatternId": "n5-005",
  "type": "sentence_order",
  "prompt_ja": "ただしい じゅんに ならべてください。",
  "tiles": ["わたしは", "がくせいです", "の", "だいがく"],
  "correctOrder": ["わたしは", "だいがく", "の", "がくせいです"],
  "translation_en": "I am a university student.",
  "explanation_en": "の links nouns; だいがくの がくせい = university's student."
}

[Heading 1] 8) System Architecture (LOCKED)
[Heading 2] 8.1 Tech Stack - Vanilla, No Build Step
[List Bullet] HTML + CSS + Vanilla JavaScript only.
[List Bullet] No npm, no bundler, no toolchain. Anyone with a modern browser can clone the repo, double-click `index.html`, and run.
[List Bullet] GitHub Pages serves the same files as the local file system.
[List Bullet] ES modules via `<script type="module">` - modern browsers support imports natively.
[List Bullet] All paths relative.
[List Bullet] Hash routing for SPA refresh safety: `/#/learn`, `/#/test`, `/#/drill`, `/#/review`, `/#/summary`.
[Heading 2] 8.2 Why this stack
[List Bullet] Zero install for the learner - just a browser.
[List Bullet] Zero install for the content author - edit JSON / MD in any editor; refresh browser.
[List Bullet] Stable across Chrome / Edge / Firefox versions; no breaking-change surface from build tools.
[List Bullet] GitHub Pages compatible without any actions or workflows.
[List Bullet] No external CDN dependencies - everything is in the repo (resilient on flaky networks).
[Heading 2] 8.3 Content Pipeline (One-Time per Edit)
[List Bullet] Source-of-truth files: `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md`, `KnowledgeBank/kanji_n5.md`, `KnowledgeBank/vocabulary_n5.md`, `KnowledgeBank/sources.md` (human-readable).
[List Bullet] App-consumed files: `/data/grammar.json`, `/data/questions.json`, `/data/n5_kanji_whitelist.json`, `/data/n5_vocab_whitelist.json`.
[List Bullet] `tools/build_data.py` - one-time conversion script (run by author after editing .md files). Generates JSON whitelists from kanji_n5.md and vocabulary_n5.md.
[List Bullet] `tools/check_coverage.py` - verifies every pattern in grammar_n5.md has an entry in grammar.json.
[List Bullet] `tools/lint_content.py` - flags non-N5 vocabulary or kanji in grammar.json / questions.json.
[List Bullet] These scripts are author-side only. The learner never runs Python.
[Heading 1] 9) Repository Structure
/JLPT/N5/                        # repo root for this app
  index.html                     # entry point - open this in browser
  /css/
    main.css
  /js/
    app.js                       # router + chapter coordinator
    learn.js                     # Chapter 1
    test.js                      # Chapter 2
    review.js                    # Chapter 3
    summary.js                   # Chapter 4
    drill.js                     # Drill / SRS engine
    diagnostic.js                # First-run diagnostic
    storage.js                   # LocalStorage adapter
    furigana.js                  # Furigana renderer + toggle
  /data/
    grammar.json                 # all N5 patterns (rich content)
    questions.json               # question bank
    n5_kanji_whitelist.json      # generated from kanji_n5.md
    n5_vocab_whitelist.json      # generated from vocabulary_n5.md
  /tools/
    build_data.py                # author tool - md to json whitelists
    check_coverage.py            # author tool - pattern coverage check
    lint_content.py              # author tool - N5 scope lint
  KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md                  # source-of-truth pattern catalog
  KnowledgeBank/kanji_n5.md                    # source-of-truth N5 kanji list
  KnowledgeBank/vocabulary_n5.md               # source-of-truth N5 vocab list
  KnowledgeBank/sources.md                     # reference / authority documentation
  JLPT N5 Grammar Tutor – Functional Spec.docx   # this file
  README.md

[Heading 1] 10) Deployment Requirements (GitHub Pages)
[List Bullet] DR-1: Static site - no build step required.
[List Bullet] DR-2: Deploy via `/docs` folder OR `gh-pages` branch (either works for vanilla).
[List Bullet] DR-3: All paths relative.
[List Bullet] DR-4: Hash routing for refresh safety.
[List Bullet] DR-5: No external CDN dependencies - every asset self-hosted in repo.
[List Bullet] DR-6: Service worker (optional Phase 1.5) for full offline capability after first load.
[Heading 1] 11) Non-Functional Requirements
[List Bullet] Performance: First load under 2s on typical broadband; subsequent loads near-instant from cache.
[List Bullet] Accessibility: Full keyboard navigation; visible focus rings; WCAG AA contrast; skip-to-main-content link.
[List Bullet] Compatibility: Latest Chrome / Edge / Firefox / Safari on PC. Mobile-friendly is non-goal (Phase 2).
[List Bullet] Reliability: No data loss without explicit "Reset progress" confirmation.
[List Bullet] Content integrity: `tools/lint_content.py` flags non-N5 kanji or vocab before commit.
[List Bullet] Offline: After first load, app continues to work offline (everything served from filesystem / LocalStorage).
[List Bullet] No telemetry / no tracking - no analytics calls, no external resources.
[Heading 1] 12) Validation & Acceptance Criteria (Must Pass)
[Heading 2] Lesson Acceptance (Chapter 1)
[List Bullet] TOC lists every pattern in `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md`, grouped by its 23 categories.
[List Bullet] Every pattern page shows the 7-block expanded template (§4.3).
[List Bullet] Furigana default is OFF on N5 kanji and ON for out-of-scope kanji; toggle persists across sessions.
[List Bullet] "Mark as known" checkbox works and updates the rolling history.
[Heading 2] Test Acceptance (Chapter 2)
[List Bullet] Submit visible-but-disabled until all questions answered, with tooltip + remaining-count.
[List Bullet] Submit click immediately renders score / answer review / weak list.
[List Bullet] Sentence-ordering question type works (click-to-order tiles, array-equality grading).
[List Bullet] Question sampling balances across categories (max ceil(N/5) per category, N ≥ 8).
[Heading 2] Diagnostic (NEW)
[List Bullet] First-run prompt is shown once and skippable.
[List Bullet] 10 questions, sampled across the highest-frequency categories.
[List Bullet] Results seed the weak list but do not count toward score history.
[Heading 2] Drill (NEW)
[List Bullet] Patterns due today shown with a badge count on the Drill tab.
[List Bullet] 1d / 3d / 7d / 14d intervals advance correctly on right answers; reset to 1d on wrong.
[List Bullet] Pattern graduates after 4 consecutive correct.
[List Bullet] Drill session shows immediate feedback per question (not batched).
[Heading 2] Review Acceptance (Chapter 3)
[List Bullet] Weak patterns shown by rolling threshold (≥ 50% error AND ≥ 2 attempts).
[List Bullet] Each weak pattern includes form rules + common mistakes + 4-6 fresh examples (different from Chapter 1).
[List Bullet] Per-distractor explanations shown for missed high-confusion questions.
[Heading 2] Summary Acceptance (Chapter 4)
[List Bullet] Mastered / weak / untested all visible.
[List Bullet] Heatmap-style category grid renders.
[List Bullet] Suggested next step is actionable (links to the right chapter / mode).
[Heading 2] Content Integrity
[List Bullet] `tools/check_coverage.py` exits 0 - every pattern in `KnowledgeBank/grammar_n5.md` has a matching `/data/grammar.json` entry.
[List Bullet] `tools/lint_content.py` exits 0 - no out-of-scope kanji/vocab in JSON without explicit furigana annotation.
[Heading 1] 13) Future Enhancements (Phase 2+)
[List Bullet] Adaptive testing with Anki-style ease factors (replaces Leitner-light).
[List Bullet] Audio on hover for furigana / pattern reading (TTS or recorded).
[List Bullet] Print / PDF export of lesson chapters.
[List Bullet] Multi-learner profiles (separate LocalStorage namespaces; or backend).
[List Bullet] N4 expansion (separate content set; same engine).
[List Bullet] Service worker for full offline + installable PWA.
[List Bullet] Mobile-friendly responsive layout.
[List Bullet] Listening practice (out of Phase 1 scope).
[Heading 1] 14) Implementation Notes (Developer Guidance)
[List Bullet] Prefer dropdown fill-in-blank over free text to avoid ambiguous grading.
[List Bullet] Sentence-ordering tiles must be word/phrase chunks, NOT individual particles. Don't make the learner rearrange `わ・た・し・は` - that's grading kana, not grammar.
[List Bullet] Every question MUST be tagged with exactly one `grammarPatternId` to support gap analysis.
[List Bullet] Mark `high_confusion: true` only when ≥ 2 distractors are pedagogically defensible (worth explaining); otherwise leave `distractor_explanations` empty.
[List Bullet] LocalStorage key namespace: `jlpt-n5-tutor:*`.
[List Bullet] Furigana renderer: use `<ruby>...<rt>...</rt></ruby>` for true semantic ruby, not parentheses.
[List Bullet] All Japanese strings in code/data must be UTF-8 (no Shift-JIS / cp932).
[List Bullet] When generating Drill sessions, prefer questions the learner has missed before - but if none exist for a due pattern, sample any question for that pattern.
[List Bullet] Keep `js/` modules small (one chapter / mode per file). Aim < 300 LOC per file before splitting.

[Heading 3] B.6 Non-functional requirements - additions
B.6.1 Internationalization
[List Bullet] NFR-I1 UI shall be available in 5 locales: en (v1 reference), vi, id, ne, zh.
[List Bullet] NFR-I2 All translatable strings shall live in locales/*.json; no hardcoded UI strings in JS modules.
[List Bullet] NFR-I3 Locale files shall share an identical key tree; missing keys fall back to English with a console warning.
[List Bullet] NFR-I4 Japanese content (lessons, examples, questions) is never translated. Furigana, kanji, and example sentences remain Japanese regardless of UI locale. Only chrome / explanations / glosses translate.
[List Bullet] NFR-I5 Locale preference persists in localStorage.uiLang and survives reload.
[List Bullet] NFR-I6 Locale switch shall not require a page reload.
B.6.2 PWA
[List Bullet] NFR-W1 Installable PWA via manifest at manifest.webmanifest; Lighthouse PWA score = 100.
[List Bullet] NFR-W2 Service worker jlpt-n5-tutor-v<N> (current: v24) uses stale-while-revalidate for the shell (HTML/CSS/JS) and cache-first for content (data/audio/locales).
[List Bullet] NFR-W3 When new shell bytes are detected, SW shall post SW_UPDATE_AVAILABLE to clients; UI shall surface a non-blocking "Update available" toast.
[List Bullet] NFR-W4 App shall remain functional offline after first load; offline indicator chip in top-right toggles with navigator.onLine.
[List Bullet] NFR-W5 App icons (192px, 512px) shall render on mobile home screen; iOS shows the icon on splash (not white screen).
[List Bullet] NFR-W6 Install banner via beforeinstallprompt; one-time, dismissible, persists in localStorage.
B.6.3 Performance (measurable budgets)

These supersede v3's vague "First load under 2s."
B.6.4 Accessibility (specific targets)
[List Bullet] NFR-A1 Conformance target: WCAG 2.1 Level AA.
[List Bullet] NFR-A2 axe-core shall report zero serious or critical violations on Home, Learn, Test, Review, Settings (per ui-testing-plan §5).
[List Bullet] NFR-A3 All interactive elements have a visible focus ring on keyboard focus.
[List Bullet] NFR-A4 Skip-to-content link shall work from keyboard.
[List Bullet] NFR-A5 prefers-reduced-motion shall disable non-essential animations; user override available in Settings.
[List Bullet] NFR-A6 Forced-colors / Windows High Contrast mode: all visible text remains visible; no information conveyed by color alone.
[List Bullet] NFR-A7 Furigana ruby is announced correctly by NVDA + Japanese voice (e.g., 学校 reads as がっこう, not がく-こう).
[List Bullet] NFR-A8 Tap targets ≥ 44×44 px (WCAG 2.5.5).
[Heading 3] B.7 Test strategy
[List Bullet] TS-1 Engine-layer tests in tests.html (currently 37/37 passing) cover SM-2 SRS math, storage round-trip, furigana renderer, grading.
[List Bullet] TS-2 UI-level testing follows the catalog at feedback/ui-testing-plan.md (22 perspectives, three execution tiers: P0 smoke 5 min / P1 gate 60 min / P2 regression 4 hours).
[List Bullet] TS-3 Japanese language accuracy is the foundational concern - testing-plan §12 is the highest-priority section. CI invariants (§12.1) are release blockers.
[List Bullet] TS-4 Content audit cadence: every quarter or after any 10+ KB-edit batch, run a Pass-N re-audit per §D below.
[List Bullet] TS-5 No release ships if any of: (a) tests.html failing, (b) §12.1 invariants failing, (c) Lighthouse Performance/Accessibility below target, (d) any open CRITICAL audit finding.
[Heading 3] B.8 Risks register

[Heading 3] B.9 Open questions / decisions log (triaged 2026-04-30)

[Heading 3] B.10 Maintenance and support model
[List Bullet] Content errors (typos, wrong readings, ungrammatical sentences): Fix in KnowledgeBank/*.md first (source of truth); regenerate data/*.json via tools/build_data.py; log as a Pass-N audit entry in verification.md. Severity CRITICAL → block release; HIGH → next release; MEDIUM/LOW → batch into next quarterly Pass.
[List Bullet] Engine bugs: File in repo issues; reproduce in tests.html; fix; add regression test.
[List Bullet] A11y regressions: Treat as P1 (block release).
[List Bullet] Cadences:
[List Bullet] Daily: nothing required; static site
[List Bullet] Per release: P0 smoke + P1 gate from ui-testing-plan §17
[List Bullet] Quarterly: Pass-N re-audit (§D below)
[List Bullet] Annually: full FSD review and amendment
[Heading 3] B.11 Release process
[List Bullet] Each release advances the SW version (jlpt-n5-tutor-v<N>) so update toast fires for clients on older shells.
[List Bullet] CHANGELOG.md is appended with a new entry describing user-visible changes.
[List Bullet] Tag the commit as content-v<N> if KB content changed; app-v<N> if engine/UI changed.
[List Bullet] No release ships with TS-5 conditions failing.
[Heading 3] B.12 Copy voice contract — added 2026-04-30 (supersedes brief2 tagline language)
The home-page hero originally followed UX brief2 §1.1 (feedback/jlpt-n5-tutor-ux-developer-brief2.md line 30, 34, 367), which prescribed an outcome-promise tagline (Pass JLPT N5 with 15 minutes a day…) and a ✓ Works offline ✓ No login required ✓ Your progress stays on this device trust strip. That language was inherited verbatim and shipped through v1.5.0.
Decision 2026-04-30: voice was reviewed by senior-copywriter lens (TASKS.md "Copy audit: remove sales-promo voice"). Brief2's tagline+trust-strip prescriptions are formally superseded by this contract:
[List Number] No outcome promises (don't say what the user will achieve).
[List Number] No time-to-result claims ("in 15 minutes a day", "quickly", "easily").
[List Number] No second-person imperatives at the brand level (Start your first lesson → Start a lesson).
[List Number] No celebration glyphs (✓-prefixed badges, ★ Graduated).
[List Number] No defensive claims ("No login required" is defensive phrasing — describe properties plainly).
[List Number] No gamification at the surface ("Keep your streak alive" → just show the count).
[List Number] Prefer noun phrases to headlines ("Today's review queue" → "Reviews due today").
[List Number] One register across all microcopy (no mixing onboarding-funnel with neutral).
The active reference for tagline/CTA/microcopy text is now (a) the live js/home.js, js/summary.js, js/drill.js, js/counters.js, and index.html, and (b) the rewrite table in TASKS.md "Copy audit". When any future doc cites brief2 §1.1 or §15 for tagline/copy, this section is the override.
Trust-strip handling: removed from the page body in v1.6.1. The same facts are now reachable via the Privacy link in the footer (which serves PRIVACY.md). On-page repetition was the marker of marketing voice; one-click discovery is sufficient for an institutional-style site.
Pillar count: Practice and Review no longer appear in the primary nav or in the home pillar grid. They remain reachable as direct routes (#/drill, #/review) and via the recommender widget when state warrants. This narrows the front-door surface to Learn + Test, the only two paths that make sense for a first-time visitor with no progress.
Brief2 section explicit overrides (added 2026-05-01): this contract overrides — *by name* — the following sections of feedback/jlpt-n5-tutor-ux-developer-brief2.md:
[List Bullet] Brief2 §1.1.5 (Trust strip) prescribed ✓ Works offline · ✓ No login required · ✓ Your progress stays on this device as a hero-band trust strip. Override: removed entirely in v1.6.1; same facts remain at PRIVACY.md linked from the footer. Do not revive the in-page strip.
[List Bullet] Brief2 §15 (Copy revisions) prescribed an outcome-promise tagline + a Pass-JLPT-in-N-minutes hero headline. Override: removed entirely in v1.7.1 (no hero, no tagline, no marketing copy on the home page). The home page now opens directly into the section-grid (Learn / Test numbered pillar cards). Do not revive the hero.
[List Bullet] Brief2 §11.4-11.6 (Reduce noise / Sentence case / Drop celebratory checkmarks) are still active and were applied in the v1.7.11 emoji cleanup + v1.8.0 Zen Modern design overhaul. The design system at specifications/jlpt-n5-design-system-zen-modern.md is now the authoritative source for visual + voice discipline.
Current home-page state (post-v1.8.0): the home shows zero marketing copy. First-time visitors see a "Sections" hairline-rule label followed by 2 numbered pillar cards (01 Learn / 02 Test) and a single-sentence diagnostic-link footnote. Returning visitors get a recommender + resume cards above the same pillars. No hero, no tagline, no trust strip, no celebration glyphs.
[Heading 3] B.13 External-blocked items reframed — added 2026-05-01 (deep-research result)
Four items were originally classified "external-blocked" with the assumption that they required human-only resources (native voice talent, native reviewer time, Japan-based outreach, learner-base data collection). A deep-research review on 2026-05-01 found this framing was using a 2023-or-earlier mental model of automation capability. The 2026 picture:

The reframing: these items moved from "human-required for the work" to "human-optional for prestige + spot-checks." The technical bar is now reachable by code. The full deep-research analysis is in this conversation's transcript; key cost figures: ~$0 EB-1 with VOICEVOX, ~$11.50 + 3hr EB-2, ~$1 + 2hr EB-3, $0 EB-4. Combined: ~$15 + ~20hr to close all four at the technical level.
What still genuinely needs humans:
[List Bullet] Native sign-off on released content (institutional / MEXT-alignment value, not technical)
[List Bullet] Cultural-appropriateness review (~10% of content)
[List Bullet] Final UI tone consistency review
These remain in the spec as native-reviewer responsibilities; they are smaller and more focused than the original "full coverage" stance.
[Heading 3] B.14 Pass 15a — free heuristic audit (2026-05-01)
A free, deterministic alternative to the LLM-audit pipeline ran on all 187 grammar patterns. Tooling: tools/heuristic_audit.py. Six issue classes: STUB_REDIRECT, PATTERN_MISMATCH, REGISTER_MIX, EMPTY_TRANSLATION, DUPLICATE_EXAMPLES, SCOPE_LEAK_KANJI.
Result: 60 findings, 75% precision (45 real / 15 heuristic-noise from verb-conjugation form matching and non-register-topical mixed examples). All 45 real findings fixed in the same pass.
Standout closures:
[List Bullet] 38 patterns had auto-generated "Duplicate-cleanup redirect. See n5-XXX..." text leaking to learners as notes. Same class as Pass-12 F-12.3 fixed for data/questions.json; the data/grammar.json equivalent had been missed.
[List Bullet] n5-158 was teaching でしょう in a pattern explicitly named 〜だろう (casual form). Pedagogical inversion sat in data through 14 prior passes.
[List Bullet] n5-112 was demonstrating its kana-counter pattern with 分 kanji examples, bypassing the learning point.
Heuristic precision per rule: H1 STUB_REDIRECT 97%, H2 PATTERN_MISMATCH 45%, H3 REGISTER_MIX 20%. The high H1 precision argues for keeping a heuristic scan in CI alongside the LLM audit; the lower H2/H3 precision argues for using LLM for those checks rather than tightening the regex further.
Full Pass-15a log in verification.md. Cumulative tally across all passes: ~635 findings, ~620 fixed, 2 deferred to Pass-15-true.
[Heading 3] B.15 Microinteractions vs Zen Modern — formal spec deviation (2026-05-01)
The original UI design brief (feedback/jlpt-n5-ui-design-brief.md §8.2) called for the following microinteractions:
[List Bullet] Card hover lift (translateY -2px on hover)
[List Bullet] Button press scale (transform: scale(0.98) on :active)
[List Bullet] Shake animation on incorrect answer
[List Bullet] Pulse / glow on correct answer
The v1.8.0 Zen Modern design overhaul (specifications/jlpt-n5-design-system-zen-modern.md §0.5 + §8) explicitly forbids these:
> §0.5: "No shadows, no gradients, no glass effects. Ever. Including hover states."
>
> §8: Forbidden motion: bouncy springs, confetti, card lift / shadow on hover, wiggle, shake, pulse, glow, sliding panes, fading in entire pages from blank.
Resolution (this section is the formal record): the Zen Modern spec supersedes the UI design brief on microinteractions. Hover affordances are rendered through:
[List Number] Background lightening (surface → surface-alt)
[List Number] Border-color strengthening (line → line-strong)
[List Number] Underline on .card-action text only
Correctness feedback in drill / test surfaces uses color + class change only — .choice-button.correct-choice gets color: var(--color-correct) + tinted background; .choice-button.wrong-choice gets the incorrect tint + strikethrough. No motion, no shake.
The Pass-15 design-system static checker (tools/check_design_system.py) enforces this:
[List Bullet] D-3 No box-shadow: declarations (other than none)
[List Bullet] D-4 No transform: inside :hover blocks
[List Bullet] D-8 No decorative text-shadow
Any future re-introduction of the UI design brief's microinteractions would fail D-3, D-4, or D-8 in CI. This guards the Zen Modern aesthetic against drift back to the SaaS-default microinteraction palette.
OPEN-9 from feedback/MASTER-TASK-LIST.md closed via this section.

[Heading 2] C. Updates to existing v3 sections (errata)
[Heading 3] C.1 §3 Scope - move out of "Out of Scope"
These were listed as out-of-scope in v3 but have shipped:

[Heading 3] C.4 §5.8 Drill / SRS - replace Leitner with SM-2
v3 says "Leitner-light, 1d / 3d / 7d / 14d intervals." Reality: the app uses SM-2 with Again / Hard / Good / Easy 4-button grading and a per-item ease factor (verified in tests.html).
Replacement text:
> Drill / Review uses SuperMemo-2 (SM-2) spaced repetition. Each item carries an ease factor (default 2.5, floor 1.3) and a current interval. Grade buttons:
> - Again (lapse): interval = 1 day; ease drops by 0.2; repetition counter resets to 0.
> - Hard: interval = max(1d, current × 1.2); ease drops by 0.15.
> - Good: per SM-2 schedule (rep 1 → 1d, rep 2 → 6d, rep 3+ → previous × ease).
> - Easy: interval = current × ease × 1.3; ease rises by 0.15.
>
> Verified in tests.html: rep 1 → 1d, rep 2 → 6d, rep 3 → 15d; lapse drops EF to 1.96.

§7.6 knownKanji set
{
  "knownKanji": ["学", "校", "本"]
}

§7.7 Streak
{
  "streak": {
    "current": 7,
    "longest": 23,
    "lastStudyDate": "2026-04-30",

}

days[] is a rolling 30-day array used by the heatmap.

data/audio_manifest.json enumerates the rendered MP3s. Format:

  "n5-001.0": "audio/n5-001.0.mp3",
  "reading-001": "audio/reading-001.mp3"
}

If a key is absent, the app degrades gracefully (no audio button).

[Heading 2] F. Revision 2026-05-03 — Mobile UI / Disabled-button feedback / Data audit / State-machine fixes
This block captures work shipped between the supplement's original 2026-04-30 revision and 2026-05-03. Each subsection is a candidate for direct merge into v4 §11 (NFRs) / §5 (UX) / §12 (Quality gates) / §13 (Future). Acceptance criteria #11 above gates the merge.
[Heading 3] F.1 Mobile UI contract — new NFR cluster (NFR-M1..NFR-M9)
The mobile experience hardening pass shipped a desktop-safe contract that gates every mobile-specific style behind explicit breakpoints and verifies desktop is byte-identical after each sweep. The contract is normative for any future mobile work in this codebase.
Breakpoint tier:

Non-functional requirements:
[List Bullet] NFR-M1 Tap-target floor — every interactive element ≥ 44 px on its smallest dimension. Auxiliary controls (icon-btn, breadcrumb back-link, footer-nav links, pattern-nav arrows, known-toggle) carry mobile-only min-height: 44px rules so the global floor reaches them.
[List Bullet] NFR-M2 iOS auto-zoom prevention — every text/search/email/number input + textarea + select rendered at ≥ 16 px on ≤768 breakpoint. Required because iOS Safari auto-zooms on tap when the input's computed font-size is below 16 px, pushing the input above the keyboard fold.
[List Bullet] NFR-M3 Overflow safety belt — html, body { overflow-x: hidden; } at ≤768 so any descendant wider than viewport never triggers page-wide horizontal scroll on a 320 px phone. Defensive; current state has zero overflow at 320 px.
[List Bullet] NFR-M4 Body type-floor — body { font-size: max(16px, var(--text-base)); } at ≤768. Respects the user's font-size knob (Settings > Font: S/M/L/XL); on default S/M (where --text-base resolves to 13–15 px), this lifts body to 16 px for readability and reinforces NFR-M2 by making body text ineligible for the iOS-zoom threshold trigger.
[List Bullet] NFR-M5 Visible tap feedback — every primary tap target gets a brief :active { transform: scale(0.97); opacity: 0.85; transition: 80ms } at ≤768. Mobile has no :hover equivalent; without this, tap-down feels unresponsive on slow networks.
[List Bullet] NFR-M6 Smooth scroll — html { scroll-behavior: smooth; } at ≤768. Anchor jumps + route changes animate; matches OS-level scroll snap.
[List Bullet] NFR-M7 Container gutter — at ≤480 breakpoint, main + .app-footer + .app-header collapse inline padding from desktop's 22.5 px to 16 px so cards use ~92 % of viewport width. Don't apply universal * { max-width: 100% } — it constrains SVG icons + brand-mark pseudo-elements.
[List Bullet] NFR-M8 Heading hierarchy on narrow screens — h2 clamps at 18 px (not desktop's 22 px) at ≤768. On 320 px viewports, 22 px h2 reads as indistinguishable from h1 (also 22 px) and the typographic cascade collapses. 22 / 18 / 16 (h1 / h2 / body) restores readable hierarchy.
[List Bullet] NFR-M9 Desktop safety — every mobile sweep ships with a 1280 px regression check. Every value listed above MUST match the desktop default after the sweep (body overflow-x: visible, scroll-behavior: auto, body font: ~14.06 px, main padding: 22.5 px, no :active rule applied, h2 at 22 px). The mobile rules MUST cut off cleanly at 769 px.
QA contract (added to ui-testing-plan §17 — Mobile QA tier):
Test at viewports 320 / 360 / 390 (the spec QA tier) plus 480 / 768 (boundary) plus 1280 (desktop safety):
[ ] No horizontal scroll  (document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth)
[ ] No CJK per-char break  (any heading wrapping with avg < 3 chars/line)
[ ] All tap targets ≥ 44 px on smallest dimension
[ ] body { font-size } ≥ 16 px at all mobile widths
[ ] All inputs ≥ 16 px (iOS zoom test)
[ ] Card descriptions show full text or clean line-aligned ellipsis (no mid-line clip per F.4 below)
[ ] Smooth scroll on anchor jumps
[ ] Desktop @ 1280: every value listed in NFR-M9 matches pre-mobile-sweep defaults

[Heading 3] F.2 Disabled-button feedback contract — new NFR-U cluster
Any control that can be disabled MUST display the reason in visible UI text — not only in a title tooltip. Tooltips don't fire on touch; mobile users get a silent dead control.
Patterns:

Saved-toast pattern (silent settings):
For settings that save but have no immediate visible side-effect (daily-new-card limit, daily-review-cap, default test length, audio-rate, reduce-motion), js/settings.js shows a brief on-screen toast on every change: Saved: <label> = <value>. CSS class .settings-saved-toast — fixed-position bottom-centre, 180 ms fade, 1800 ms auto-dismiss, respects prefers-reduced-motion.
Export action confirmation: the file dialog is easily missed in the browser's downloads bar. js/settings.js shows a status line under the Export button: Exported to <filename> (check your downloads folder). Auto-clears after 4 s.
NFR-U1 Pre-release feedback audit checklist:
[ ] Every <button [...] disabled> rendering site has visible reason text
[ ] Every settings setter without immediate visual effect has a saved-toast
[ ] Every async action with > 200 ms latency has a loading or status indicator
[ ] Every disabled control verified by tap (not hover) on a touch-only browser

[Heading 3] F.3 New invariants — JA-25..JA-33
Adds nine invariants to the §C.7 / tools/check_content_integrity.py register beyond the JA-21 baseline of v3.1:

The release-blocker rule (NFR-C, §C.7) extends to the new invariants: all 33 must pass before any release.
[Heading 3] F.4 New anti-patterns from N5 layout regressions
Documented in the procedure manual at procedure-manual-build-next-jlpt-level.md §3.2.7 – §3.2.10; reference here for in-spec record:
[List Bullet] AP-7 Don't ship cross-references to retired patterns after a dedup pass (HIGH). After any pattern-retirement pass, scan for contrasts.with_pattern_id and form_rules.conjugations.label "See n5-XXX" references to retired IDs; repoint to canonical replacement or remove. JA-32 invariant catches this going forward.
[List Bullet] AP-8 Don't mass-stamp PoS by thematic vocab section (CRITICAL). The Group-2 case is pedagogically dangerous — a learner using あげる from the existence-section copy gets told it's verb-1 → conjugates *あげります instead of あげます. Tag PoS per-WORD, not per-section default. Closed in Pass-24 (23+ entries fixed). JA-31 invariant catches regressions.
[List Bullet] AP-9 Don't mid-line-clip card descriptions on fixed-height tile grids (HIGH). flex: 1 + display: -webkit-box + -webkit-line-clamp: N + fixed-height parent = Chrome normalises display to flow-root, line-clamp goes inert, overflow:hidden mid-line-clips. Belt-and-suspenders fix: max-height: Nlh next to -webkit-line-clamp + remove flex: 1 + use margin-top: auto on action element to pin to card bottom. JA-33 invariant catches regressions.
[List Bullet] AP-10 Don't keep stale module-level state on URL navigation (HIGH). Render entry-points that short-circuit on view === 'finished' without resetting on URL navigation cause back-buttons inside results pages to re-render the same results page. Reset rule: 'attempting' resumes on refresh; 'finished' / 'results' resets when URL navigates away. Applied to papers.js / test.js / review.js / drill.js.

Replaces v3 §0 / §A.7 corpus counts:

[Heading 3] F.7 Service worker version + PWA contract additions
[List Bullet] NFR-W2 update: SW current = jlpt-n5-tutor-v110 (as of 2026-05-03). The version bumps on every shell change. Frequency since supplement was last edited (~2026-05-01): ~16 bumps in 2 days reflecting the Mobile UI / Data audit / State-machine sweep cadence.
[List Bullet] NFR-W7 (new): cache-bust contract for ES modules. Browsers cache ES modules by URL; bumping CACHE_VERSION in sw.js is necessary but not sufficient — index.html must also bump ?v=N on the entry script (<script src="js/app.js?v=N">). Without both, users see stale shell on next visit because the unchanged import './module-x.js' URLs hit the browser's module cache.
[List Bullet] NFR-W8 (new): SW pre-cache list maintenance. The PRECACHE array in sw.js must list every static asset reachable on first paint, including the inline-SVG favicon, the 5 locale files, all 25+ JS modules, and the 4 self-hosted woff2 font subsets. Missing entries silently degrade offline UX.
[List Bullet] §D / Content-protection layer: shipped as a feature flag in js/content-protect.js (CONTENT_PROTECT_ENABLED). Currently OFF (allows screenshots / Ctrl+C / right-click for bug-report use). When ON: blocks contextmenu / copy / cut / dragstart / selectstart / Ctrl+C/A/X/S/P/U / F12 / Ctrl+Shift+I/J/K/C; window-blur sets html[data-blur=true] to obscure during region-screenshots; @media print blanks the page. Documented as honest deterrent — devtools / view-source / phone-camera-of-screen still work.
[List Bullet] `CONTENT-LICENSE.md`: shipped at repo root. Original-content policy + reference sources + JEES contact path. JA-30 invariant + tools/audit_provenance.py enforce the policy at CI time.

[Heading 2] §G Design System (Zen Modern) — merged 2026-05-04
*Was at jlpt-n5-design-system-zen-modern.md. Merged into this supplement so the functional spec is the single source of truth for both functional + visual contracts. The original file has been deleted; this section is the canonical record going forward.*

[Heading 1] JLPT N5 Tutor - Design System: Zen Modern (Muji)
Theme: Zen Modern - Muji-inspired
Goal: Spare, generous, almost monochrome. The product earns trust by getting out of the way. Looks credible on first load. Still looks credible in three years.
Influences: Muji catalog typography, Kinokuniya book design, Apple Human Interface circa 2014-16 (pre-glassmorphism), Japanese editorial print.
This document is the source of truth. Every page, component, and interaction in the JLPT N5 Tutor must conform to these tokens and rules. Ambiguity is resolved in favor of *less* visual weight, never more.

[Heading 2] 0. Design principles (read these before writing code)
[List Number] Hierarchy through scale and weight, not color. Color is for state (correct, wrong, due). Hierarchy comes from type size and whitespace.
[List Number] One accent color, used sparingly. The brand green is reserved for the active nav indicator, the primary CTA, and the focus ring. Nowhere else.
[List Number] Whitespace is the design. When in doubt, add 24px of vertical space, not a divider.
[List Number] Hairlines, not borders. All borders are 0.5px or 1px in a near-paper gray. Never thicker.
[List Number] No shadows, no gradients, no glass effects. Ever. Including hover states.
[List Number] No emojis, no decorative icons in body text. Use small SVG glyphs only where functional (audio play, stroke order, search).
[List Number] Typography weight 300 and 400 only for body. 500 for headings. Never 600 or 700. Heavy weights look loud against this neutral surface.
[List Number] All text is sentence case. Never Title Case. Never ALL CAPS except for tiny letter-spaced labels (e.g., "REFERENCE" above a card group).
[List Number] Numerals are tabular (`font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`). Stats, counters, timers, and SRS intervals must align vertically.
[List Number] Japanese text uses Noto Sans JP weight 400. Mixing weights between latin and ja text creates uneven visual rhythm.
[List Number] Describe the contents. Offer no opinion about them. Homepage copy is neutral and inventorial. No outcome claims, no second-person, no verbs of encouragement, no trust reassurance, no superlatives, no quantifier softeners. Counts are bare numerals plus nouns. The product is a study material; the copy says what it is, not what it does for the visitor.
If a proposed change would violate any of these, the proposed change is wrong.

[Heading 2] 1. Color tokens
[Heading 3] 1.1 Light mode
:root {
  /* Surfaces */
  --color-bg:           #FFFFFF;   /* page background */
  --color-surface:      #FAFAF8;   /* card backgrounds, subtle differentiation */
  --color-surface-alt:  #F5F4F0;   /* hover state on cards */
 
  /* Hairlines */
  --color-line:         #E8E5DE;   /* default border / hairline */
  --color-line-strong:  #D4D0C7;   /* hover or focus border */
 
  /* Text */
  --color-text:         #1F1F1C;   /* primary text - near black, slight warmth */
  --color-text-muted:   #6F6D66;   /* secondary text, captions */
  --color-text-faint:   #9A968C;   /* tertiary text, hints, disabled labels */
 
  /* Brand accent (used sparingly) */
  --color-accent:       #1F4D2E;   /* deep forest green - active nav, primary CTA */
  --color-accent-hover: #163924;
  --color-accent-tint:  #EDF1EE;   /* very light tint for backgrounds of pills */
 
  /* Semantic (state only) */
  --color-correct:      #2E7D4F;
  --color-correct-tint: #ECF4EE;
  --color-incorrect:    #B43A2A;
  --color-incorrect-tint:#F7EAE7;
  --color-due:          #A66A1F;   /* warm amber for "review due" badges */
  --color-due-tint:     #F7EFE0;
}

[Heading 3] 1.2 Dark mode (mandatory parity)
@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {
  :root {
    --color-bg:           #1A1A18;
    --color-surface:      #232321;
    --color-surface-alt:  #2C2C29;
    --color-line:         #34332F;
    --color-line-strong:  #4A4944;
    --color-text:         #ECEAE3;
    --color-text-muted:   #A8A59C;
    --color-text-faint:   #6F6D66;
    --color-accent:       #6FB58A;
    --color-accent-hover: #88C7A2;
    --color-accent-tint:  #2A3A2F;
    --color-correct:      #6FB58A;
    --color-correct-tint: #233028;
    --color-incorrect:    #E08572;
    --color-incorrect-tint:#3A2723;
    --color-due:          #D9A560;
    --color-due-tint:     #332B1E;
  }
}

[Heading 3] 1.3 Color usage rules
[List Bullet] Primary text: always --color-text. Never near-black #000.
[List Bullet] Body backgrounds: always --color-bg. Cards step up to --color-surface.
[List Bullet] Hover backgrounds: step from --color-surface to --color-surface-alt. Never use the accent tint as a hover.
[List Bullet] Accent green: ONLY on (1) the active nav underline, (2) the primary CTA filled button, (3) focus rings, (4) progress-bar fills, and (5) the small accent line under the wordmark. Five places total. Audit your CSS to enforce this.
[List Bullet] Tinted pills (accent-tint, due-tint, etc.): use sparingly. Reserve for state badges and small status indicators. Never as backgrounds for cards or sections.
[List Bullet] Never use red or warning colors for anything except actual errors. A "review due" indicator is amber, not red.

[Heading 2] 2. Typography
[Heading 3] 2.1 Font stack
:root {
  /* Latin */
  --font-en:    "Inter", -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont,
                "Segoe UI", system-ui, sans-serif;
 
  /* Japanese */
  --font-jp:    "Noto Sans JP", "Hiragino Kaku Gothic ProN",
                "Yu Gothic", "Meiryo", sans-serif;
 
  /* Tabular numerics */
  --font-num:   "Inter", system-ui, sans-serif;
  /* with: font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums; */
}
 
body {
  font-family: var(--font-en);
  color: var(--color-text);
  font-feature-settings: "ss01" 1, "cv11" 1;  /* Inter alt single-story a, alt 4 */
}
 
:lang(ja), .lang-ja {
  font-family: var(--font-jp);
  font-feature-settings: "palt" 1;   /* proportional alternate widths for cleaner ja */
}

Inter is required (not optional) because the design depends on its weight 300 (Light) being available. System fallbacks don't have a true Light. If Inter fails to load, fall through to system but accept that the design will look slightly heavier.
Noto Sans JP must be loaded with weights 400 and 500 only. Subset to N5 + N4 character ranges to keep file size near 200KB woff2. Preload in <head>:
<link rel="preload" href="/fonts/noto-sans-jp-400.woff2" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin>
<link rel="preload" href="/fonts/inter-400.woff2" as="font" type="font/woff2" crossorigin>

[Heading 3] 2.2 Type scale
The scale is deliberately small. Big type fights the design.
:root {
  --text-2xs:  0.6875rem;  /* 11px - tiny labels with letter-spacing */
  --text-xs:   0.75rem;    /* 12px - footer text */
  --text-sm:   0.8125rem;  /* 13px - card body, helper text */
  --text-base: 0.9375rem;  /* 15px - body */
  --text-md:   1.0625rem;  /* 17px - emphasized body / card titles */
  --text-lg:   1.25rem;    /* 20px - section headings */
  --text-xl:   1.625rem;   /* 26px - page titles */
  --text-2xl:  2rem;       /* 32px - hero headlines (used once per page max) */
}

Note the body size (15px) is one notch smaller than the SaaS default (16px). This is intentional. It pulls the whole UI tighter and is correct for content-heavy pages.
[Heading 3] 2.3 Weight rules
:root {
  --weight-light:  300;
  --weight-base:   400;
  --weight-medium: 500;
}

[List Bullet] Body paragraphs: --weight-base
[List Bullet] Headings (h1, h2, h3): --weight-medium
[List Bullet] Page titles only: occasional use of --weight-light for the largest hero headline (--text-2xl, weight 300) - this is the Muji signature look.
[List Bullet] Japanese text: always --weight-base. Never light, never medium. Japanese forms have built-in optical weight that doesn't need typographic emphasis.
[List Bullet] Buttons: --weight-medium.
[List Bullet] Numerals in stats: --weight-medium with font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums.
[List Bullet] Never use weight 600 or 700. They look loud against this neutral surface.
[Heading 3] 2.4 Letter spacing
:root {
  --tracking-tight:  -0.01em;
  --tracking-normal: 0;
  --tracking-wide:   0.05em;
  --tracking-label:  0.18em;   /* used on tiny ALL-CAPS labels */
}

[List Bullet] Body: --tracking-normal.
[List Bullet] Headings: --tracking-tight. Inter's default tracking at large sizes is slightly too open.
[List Bullet] Tiny ALL-CAPS labels (e.g., "REFERENCE" above a card group): --tracking-label. This is the only place ALL CAPS is allowed.
[List Bullet] Japanese text: never apply letter-spacing. Japanese typography handles its own rhythm.
[Heading 3] 2.5 Line height
:root {
  --leading-tight: 1.25;
  --leading-base:  1.55;
  --leading-loose: 1.7;
}

[List Bullet] Headings: --leading-tight.
[List Bullet] Body paragraphs: --leading-base.
[List Bullet] Long-form reading content (passages, explanations): --leading-loose.
[List Bullet] Japanese paragraphs: --leading-loose minimum. Japanese needs more vertical air than English.
[Heading 3] 2.6 Headline templates
.h1 {
  font-size: var(--text-2xl);
  font-weight: var(--weight-light);
  letter-spacing: var(--tracking-tight);
  line-height: var(--leading-tight);
  margin: 0;
}
.h2 {
  font-size: var(--text-xl);
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);
  letter-spacing: var(--tracking-tight);
  line-height: var(--leading-tight);
  margin: 0;
}
.h3 {
  font-size: var(--text-lg);
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);
  letter-spacing: var(--tracking-tight);
  line-height: var(--leading-tight);
  margin: 0;
}
.label {
  font-size: var(--text-2xs);
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);
  letter-spacing: var(--tracking-label);
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--color-text-muted);
  margin: 0;
}


[Heading 2] 3. Spacing and layout
[Heading 3] 3.1 Spacing scale (4px base, 8pt-aware)
:root {
  --space-1:  0.25rem;   /*  4px */
  --space-2:  0.5rem;    /*  8px */
  --space-3:  0.75rem;   /* 12px */
  --space-4:  1rem;      /* 16px */
  --space-5:  1.5rem;    /* 24px */
  --space-6:  2rem;      /* 32px */
  --space-8:  3rem;      /* 48px */
  --space-10: 4rem;      /* 64px */
  --space-12: 6rem;      /* 96px */
  --space-16: 8rem;      /* 128px */
}

The Muji aesthetic is defined by the BIG end of this scale. Section spacing is --space-10 or --space-12, not --space-6.
[Heading 3] 3.2 Container widths
:root {
  --container-narrow: 640px;   /* drill cards, settings page, single-column reading */
  --container-base:   880px;   /* standard content width */
  --container-wide:   1120px;  /* dashboards, multi-column index pages */
}

Use --container-base as the default. Don't fight to fill the viewport; large empty side margins are part of the look.
[Heading 3] 3.3 Page rhythm
A standard page has this vertical rhythm:
[ header, sticky ]                      <-- 56px tall
[ space-10 = 64px ]
[ page title, 32px / weight 300 ]
[ space-2 = 8px ]
[ tiny "page kind" label, ALL CAPS ]
[ space-8 = 48px ]
[ first content section ]
[ space-12 = 96px between sections ]
[ next content section ]
[ space-16 = 128px before footer ]
[ footer, 40px tall ]

The 96px between major sections is non-negotiable. It's what separates this design from a generic SaaS layout where sections sit 32-48px apart.
[Heading 3] 3.4 Border radius
:root {
  --radius-sm: 2px;    /* tags, small chips */
  --radius-md: 4px;    /* buttons, inputs, small cards */
  --radius-lg: 6px;    /* large cards */
  --radius-pill: 999px;/* pills, badges */
}

Radii are intentionally small. The Muji look is geometric and slightly hard-edged. Avoid 12-16px radii common in modern SaaS.
[Heading 3] 3.5 Hairlines
All borders are 0.5px (1px on screens that don't subpixel-render):
.hairline {
  border: 0.5px solid var(--color-line);
}
@media (-webkit-min-device-pixel-ratio: 1.5) {
  /* hi-DPI: 0.5px renders correctly */
}
@media (-webkit-max-device-pixel-ratio: 1) {
  .hairline { border-width: 1px; }
}

Some browsers round 0.5px to 0px. Defensive fallback: ship border: 1px solid var(--color-line) and let the muted color do the work. The reader won't notice the difference.

The brand mark is a small 24px square box containing a Japanese character (五 = "5"). It anchors the wordmark with cultural specificity and costs nothing.
[Heading 3] 4.2 Buttons
Three variants. Use them strictly.
.btn {
  display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center;
  height: 36px;
  padding: 0 var(--space-4);
  font-size: var(--text-sm);
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);
  border-radius: var(--radius-md);
  border: 0.5px solid transparent;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: background 150ms ease, color 150ms ease, border-color 150ms ease;
  font-family: inherit;
}
.btn:focus-visible {
  outline: 2px solid var(--color-accent);
  outline-offset: 2px;
}
 
/* Primary - the action the page wants you to take. ONE per page max. */
.btn-primary {
  background: var(--color-accent);
  color: #FFFFFF;
}
.btn-primary:hover { background: var(--color-accent-hover); }
 
/* Secondary - the "alternate path" action. */
.btn-secondary {
  background: var(--color-bg);
  color: var(--color-text);
  border-color: var(--color-line);
}
.btn-secondary:hover {
  border-color: var(--color-line-strong);
  background: var(--color-surface);
}
 
/* Ghost - tertiary actions. Looks like a styled link with breathing room. */
.btn-ghost {
  background: transparent;
  color: var(--color-text-muted);
}
.btn-ghost:hover { color: var(--color-text); }
 
/* Sizes */
.btn-sm { height: 28px; padding: 0 var(--space-3); font-size: var(--text-xs); }
.btn-lg { height: 44px; padding: 0 var(--space-5); font-size: var(--text-base); }

}

Note the card hover state: the card lightens to surface-alt and the border darkens slightly. NO shadow, NO transform, NO scale. The lightening alone signals interactivity.
[Heading 3] 4.4 Numbered indicator
A signature Muji-style affordance. Use on cards in a sequence (Learn modules, Test sections).

  font-size: var(--text-xs);
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);

  margin: 0 0 var(--space-3);
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
}

The 2-digit zero-padded number does enormous work. It signals "this is part of a curated set" and creates a visual anchor at top-left of every card.

Pills are for STATUS only. Don't use them as decoration.

}

}

2px is correct. Anything thicker reads as a "feature," not a hint. The progress bar should be present but barely there.

[Heading 3] 4.8 Tables
Used for vocabulary lists, conjugation tables, kanji readings.
.table {
  width: 100%;

  font-size: var(--text-sm);
}

  text-align: left;
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);
