No vertical borders. No striping. Rows separated by hairlines only.

[Heading 2] 5. Page-by-page treatments
[Heading 3] 5.1 Home (landing)
Layout:
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ [header, sticky]                                     │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│                                                      │
│   [container 880px, centered, top margin 96px]      │
│                                                      │
│   JLPT N5 study material.                           │  <- text-2xl, weight 300
│                                                      │
│   [space-5]                                          │
│   187 grammar patterns.                              │  <- text-base, default
│   1,003 vocabulary items.                            │     weight, one line each
│   106 kanji.                                         │
│   30 reading passages.                               │
│   12 listening drills.                               │
│                                                      │
│   [space-12]                                         │
│   ── SECTIONS ─────────────────────────────────       │
│                                                      │
│   [space-6]                                          │
│   ┌─────────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────────┐│
│   │ 01                      │ │ 02                  ││
│   │                         │ │                     ││
│   │ Learn                   │ │ Test                ││
│   │ Grammar, vocabulary,    │ │ 15-question mock    ││
│   │ kanji, reading,         │ │ exam.               ││
│   │ listening.              │ │                     ││
│   │                       → │ │                   → ││
│   └─────────────────────────┘ └─────────────────────┘│
│                                                      │
│   [space-6]                                          │
│   Placement check available.                         │  <- inline link
│                                                      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Specific rules for this page:
[List Bullet] The headline is a single noun phrase terminated by a period: JLPT N5 study material. Not a verb phrase. No subtitle.
[List Bullet] Below the headline, render a five-line inventory. Each line is a numeral plus a noun phrase plus a period. Lines are stacked, not bulleted. Do not use middle-dots between items. Do not add a heading above this list.
[List Bullet] Counts in the inventory must be read at runtime from data/*.json _meta.entity_count fields (or equivalent). Do not hardcode. If a count changes by one, the homepage updates automatically.
[List Bullet] Use comma as thousands separator for 4+ digit numbers (1,003). Bare digits otherwise.
[List Bullet] No primary CTA button. No "Start your first lesson", no "Take the placement check" as a green button. The cards in the SECTIONS grid are the entry points.
[List Bullet] No trust line. Do not render Works offline · No login required · Stays on device on the homepage. Those properties live in About / Privacy.
[List Bullet] No "What's new" callout, no "New!" badges, no version chip.
[List Bullet] Returning users (when localStorage shows progress) get a single resume strip ABOVE the SECTIONS label. Format: Last session: [section name], [topic]. [N] of [M]. The whole line is a link to the resumption point. No "continue", no "resume", no exclamation. Hide the strip entirely for first-time visitors. No fallback.
[List Bullet] Section labels (SECTIONS) use .section-label from §5.1 unchanged.
[List Bullet] Two cards in the grid, full-width 50/50 on desktop, stacked on mobile. Each card has the standard structure: index number, h3 title, single-sentence body terminated by a period, → chevron right-aligned. No "Browse" text label.
[List Bullet] Below the cards: a single inline sentence Placement check available. The whole sentence is a link to /placement. Link styling uses --color-text with a hairline underline on hover only. Not browser-default purple/blue.
.section-label {
  display: flex; align-items: center; gap: var(--space-3);
  margin-bottom: var(--space-6);
}
.section-label-text {
  font-size: var(--text-2xs);
  letter-spacing: var(--tracking-label);
  text-transform: uppercase;
  color: var(--color-text-muted);
  font-weight: var(--weight-medium);
}
.section-label-rule {
  flex: 1;
  height: 0.5px;
  background: var(--color-line);
}

[Heading 3] 5.1.1 Copy register (mandatory)
The homepage is the most-seen surface in the app. Its copy sets the register for everything else. The rules:
[List Number] Describe the contents. Offer no opinion about them.
[List Number] No outcome claims. The product is a study material, not a promise.
[List Number] No second-person ("you", "your"). Use noun phrases or impersonal sentences.
[List Number] No verbs of encouragement ("start", "begin", "explore", "discover").
[List Number] No trust reassurance ("works offline", "no login", "privacy-first") on the homepage. Those properties live in About / Privacy.
[List Number] No service-encounter politeness ("please", "feel free to").
[List Number] Counts are bare numerals plus nouns. No "~1000", no "1000+", no "carefully selected", no "all".
[List Number] No superlatives: best, most comprehensive, complete, definitive.
[List Number] No flattering self-description: clean, minimal, elegant, distraction-free. The design demonstrates these; the copy must not assert them.
[List Number] Sentence case throughout. ALL CAPS only for the small letter-spaced section labels.
If a proposed homepage string violates any of these, the string is wrong.
The same register applies to all homepage variants: light mode, dark mode, mobile, returning-user, and translated locales.
[Heading 3] 5.2 Learn index
The page shown in the screenshot you uploaded. Specific changes:
[List Bullet] Drop the grouped "REFERENCE" and "PRACTICE" labels into the hairline-rule treatment from above.
[List Bullet] Numbered cards (01-05).
[List Bullet] Each card has, in vertical order: index number, h3 title, meta line (one line, no wrapping), the same 2px progress bar showing % completion of that module, then "Browse" as a small text action.
[List Bullet] The five modules in suggested visual order: Grammar (01), Vocabulary (02), Kanji (03), Reading (04), Listening (05). This is reading-frequency order, not screen-grid-symmetry order.
<article class="card card-link" href="#/learn/grammar">
  <p class="card-index">01</p>
  <h3 class="card-title">Grammar</h3>
  <p class="card-meta">187 patterns across 32 categories.</p>
  <div class="progress" style="margin-top: var(--space-3)">
    <div class="progress-fill" style="width: 42%"></div>
  </div>
  <p class="card-action">Browse</p>
</article>

[Heading 3] 5.3 Grammar pattern detail
The most type-heavy page. This is where the design earns its keep.
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ← Back to Grammar                            │  <- ghost link
│                                              │
│ TE-FORM                                      │  <- label, ALL CAPS
│ ～て                                         │  <- text-2xl, ja, weight 400
│ Connecting form for verbs                    │  <- text-md, muted
│                                              │
│ ── form ─────────────────                    │
│                                              │
│ Group 1   う/つ/る → って   買う → 買って   │  <- table
│ Group 1   む/ぬ/ぶ → んで   飲む → 飲んで   │
│ Group 1   く → いて (行く → 行って)         │
│ Group 1   ぐ → いで         泳ぐ → 泳いで   │
│ Group 1   す → して         話す → 話して   │
│ Group 2   drop る + て      食べる → 食べて │
│ Irreg     する → して, 来る → 来て           │
│                                              │
│ ── examples ────────────                     │
│                                              │
│ 1.  ご飯を食べて、コーヒーを飲みます。        │
│     I eat a meal and then drink coffee.     │
│     [▷ play audio]                           │
│                                              │
│ 2.  本を読んで、寝ます。                      │
│     I read a book and go to sleep.          │
│     [▷ play audio]                           │
│                                              │
│ ── common mistakes ──────                    │
│                                              │
│ Wrong  たべるて                               │
│ Right  たべて                                 │
│        Drop る before adding て.              │
│                                              │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

Specifics:
[List Bullet] The pattern form (～て) is rendered LARGE in --text-2xl weight 400, in a small dedicated block at the top with --space-5 of breathing room below it.
[List Bullet] "TE-FORM" small label above the form is in the page-kind label style.
[List Bullet] Section dividers use the hairline-rule pattern from §5.1 with lowercase ja-romaji labels: form, examples, common mistakes.
[List Bullet] Examples are NUMBERED (1, 2, 3) in the type, not bulleted. Use an <ol class="example-list">.
[List Bullet] Audio play affordance is a small ▷ character on its own line, muted color, treated like a footnote.
[List Bullet] Common mistakes use a 2-column micro-table: "Wrong" / "Right" labels in --color-incorrect and --color-correct, then the explanation as muted body text below.
.pattern-form {
  font-family: var(--font-jp);
  font-size: var(--text-2xl);
  font-weight: var(--weight-base);
  margin: var(--space-2) 0;
  letter-spacing: 0;
}
.example-list {
  list-style: none;
  counter-reset: example;
  padding: 0;
  margin: 0;
}
.example-list > li {
  counter-increment: example;
  padding: var(--space-4) 0;
  border-bottom: 0.5px solid var(--color-line);
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: 28px 1fr;
  gap: var(--space-3);
}
.example-list > li::before {
  content: counter(example) ".";
  color: var(--color-text-faint);
  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
  font-size: var(--text-sm);
}
.example-ja { font-family: var(--font-jp); font-size: var(--text-md); margin: 0 0 var(--space-1); }
.example-en { font-size: var(--text-sm); color: var(--color-text-muted); margin: 0; }

Notes:
[List Bullet] Vast vertical breathing room between the question and the choices: --space-12 = 96px. The empty space is the design.
[List Bullet] Keyboard hint (kbd element) shows in the corner of each choice. After the user uses the keyboard once, they're hinted forever.
[List Bullet] Feedback is INSTANT and color-only. No animations, no shake. The chosen button colors itself; the correct one (if user was wrong) colors itself green. A small explanatory text appears below.

The empty state uses --text-2xl weight 300 for "All caught up", muted body for the explanation, and a primary + secondary button row.
[Heading 3] 5.8 Test
Test mode looks subtly different from Practice. The user should feel the formality.
[List Bullet] Background of the test header is --color-surface (slightly off-white) instead of pure white. Tiny cue that this is a different mode.
[List Bullet] Timer in the header, monospaced, prominent: font-family: var(--font-num); font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;.
[List Bullet] Question card has a slightly thicker top edge (1.5px in --color-text color) to mark it as authoritative.
[List Bullet] "Submit" button replaces the "Continue" button - it's the same primary button but with that label.
[List Bullet] No immediate per-question feedback. Answers are recorded and reviewed at the end.
[Heading 3] 5.9 Summary / dashboard
This is the page that has the most numbers. The Muji discipline is to NOT show all of them.
Top of page: one big stat - total mastery percentage - in --text-2xl weight 300.
Below it: a 5-row "mastery ladder" showing each of the five modules with a 2px progress bar each.
Below that: a single "Weak spots" panel listing the user's three worst categories with a "Drill these" CTA.
Below that: a 7×53 GitHub-style heatmap of daily activity, using a single-color ramp (--color-accent-tint to --color-accent).
That's the entire page. No "achievements", no "trophies", no XP, no level. The Muji approach is "you've done this much; here's what's weak; go practice."
[Heading 3] 5.10 Settings
Single-column, max-width 640px. Sectioned by category. Each setting on its own row, label on left, control on right, with a hairline below.
.setting-row {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: 1fr auto;
  align-items: center;
  gap: var(--space-4);
  padding: var(--space-4) 0;
  border-bottom: 0.5px solid var(--color-line);
}
.setting-label { font-size: var(--text-base); font-weight: var(--weight-base); margin: 0; }
.setting-help { font-size: var(--text-sm); color: var(--color-text-muted); margin: 4px 0 0; }
.setting-control { justify-self: end; }

Destructive actions (Reset progress) at the bottom, separated by --space-8 of whitespace, with a thin top border in --color-incorrect and a small label "Danger zone" in muted text.

<footer class="app-footer">

The version number must be read at runtime (e.g., from package.json or a build-time constant), not hardcoded. The "Updated [Month Year]" suffix from earlier drafts is dropped — the changelog link replaces that affordance. "What's new" links to the in-app #/changelog route which renders the markdown changelog with the app's typography; never to a raw .md file on GitHub.
.app-footer {

[Heading 2] 6. Furigana
Use semantic <ruby>:
<ruby>日本語<rt>にほんご</rt></ruby>

ruby { ruby-position: over; }
ruby rt {
  font-size: 0.55em;
  color: var(--color-text-muted);
  font-weight: var(--weight-base);
  font-family: var(--font-jp);
  letter-spacing: 0;
}
.furigana-off rt { display: none; }
.furigana-known rt { visibility: hidden; }   /* preserves space, hides reading */

The toggle hides ruby via CSS class on <body>. No re-render needed.

[Heading 2] 7. Iconography
Use a small, consistent SVG icon set. Recommendation: lucide-icons subset (search, settings, play, pause, chevron-right, x, check, alert-circle, refresh-cw). Stroke width 1.5px to match the hairline aesthetic; the default 2px lucide stroke is too heavy.
svg.icon {
  width: 16px; height: 16px;
  stroke-width: 1.5;
  color: currentColor;
}
svg.icon-sm { width: 14px; height: 14px; }
svg.icon-lg { width: 20px; height: 20px; }

No icon should be larger than 24px in any UI surface except the kanji-tile glyph (which isn't an icon, it's content).
NO emojis. NO decorative icons in body text. NO colored icons.

[Heading 2] 8. Motion
Movement is minimal and purposeful.
:root {
  --motion-fast:   120ms;
  --motion-base:   180ms;
  --motion-slow:   300ms;
  --easing:        cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0, 1);   /* iOS-like ease-out */
}
@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  *, *::before, *::after {
    animation-duration: 0.01ms !important;
    transition-duration: 0.01ms !important;
  }
}

Allowed motion:
[List Bullet] Hover state transitions: --motion-fast ease-out on background and border
[List Bullet] Button press: scale to 0.98 for --motion-fast
[List Bullet] Progress bar fill: --motion-slow width transition
[List Bullet] Page transitions: cross-fade only, --motion-base. No slide, no zoom.
Forbidden motion:
[List Bullet] Bouncy springs
[List Bullet] Confetti
[List Bullet] Card lift / shadow on hover
[List Bullet] Wiggle, shake, pulse, glow
[List Bullet] Sliding panes
[List Bullet] Fading in entire pages from blank

[Heading 2] 9. Imagery and ornament
The Muji approach uses almost no imagery. What it permits:
[List Bullet] A single Japanese character as a brand mark or watermark (very low opacity). Used once on the home hero, optionally.
[List Bullet] The kanji glyphs in the Kanji index are the only "imagery" most pages need.
[List Bullet] Stroke-order diagrams in kanji detail pages: SVG, single-stroke, animated only on user click.
Forbidden:
[List Bullet] Photos of any kind (people studying, Tokyo skylines, sushi).
[List Bullet] Stock illustrations.
[List Bullet] Decorative patterns or textures.
[List Bullet] Backgrounds with visible color (everything is --color-bg or --color-surface).
[List Bullet] Brand mascot.
[List Bullet] Cherry blossoms. Especially cherry blossoms.
The product is the type, the spacing, and the hairlines. Adding decoration weakens the design.

[Heading 2] 10. Responsive behavior
[Heading 3] 10.1 Breakpoints
/* Mobile first. Defaults are mobile. */
:root { /* mobile defaults */ }
 
@media (min-width: 600px) { /* tablet */ }
@media (min-width: 960px) { /* desktop */ }
@media (min-width: 1280px) { /* wide desktop */ }

[Heading 3] 10.2 Mobile rules
[List Bullet] Header height shrinks to 48px.
[List Bullet] Primary nav becomes a bottom tab bar on mobile.
[List Bullet] Card grids stack to single column.
[List Bullet] All cards retain their numbered index (it works at any width).
[List Bullet] Drill choices stay in a 2x2 grid (don't stack vertically).
[List Bullet] Vocab two-column layout collapses: rail becomes a horizontal scrollable chip strip at top.
[List Bullet] Footer stacks vertically.
[List Bullet] Page padding reduces from 32px to 20px on mobile.
[Heading 3] 10.3 Tap targets
Every interactive element must be at least 44x44 CSS pixels. Don't shrink buttons on mobile; shrink the layout around them.

[Heading 2] 11. Accessibility
Non-negotiable. WCAG 2.1 AA.
[List Bullet] Color contrast: minimum 4.5:1 for body text against background. The token combinations above are pre-verified.
[List Bullet] Focus rings: every interactive element has a visible focus ring (2px accent color, 2px offset).
[List Bullet] Keyboard navigation: tab order matches visual order. Esc closes modals. Arrow keys cycle drill choices.
[List Bullet] Screen readers: every Japanese text container has lang="ja". Furigana via <ruby> works correctly with VoiceOver/NVDA.
[List Bullet] Motion: all animations respect prefers-reduced-motion.
[List Bullet] Form labels: every input has an associated <label>.
[List Bullet] Headings: one <h1> per page; nested headings don't skip levels.

[Heading 2] 12. Implementation checklist
For the developer building this:
[List Bullet] [ ] Set up CSS custom properties in :root with all tokens from §1, §2, §3.
[List Bullet] [ ] Implement dark mode media query block with full parity (§1.2).
[List Bullet] [ ] Load Inter (300, 400, 500) and Noto Sans JP (400, 500) via woff2.
[List Bullet] [ ] Subset Noto Sans JP to N5 + N4 character ranges.
[List Bullet] [ ] Preload critical fonts in <head>.
[List Bullet] [ ] Build header component (§4.1) and reuse on every page.
[List Bullet] [ ] Build button variants (§4.2). Audit codebase: no other button styles exist.
[List Bullet] [ ] Build card component (§4.3) with link variant.
[List Bullet] [ ] Build form controls (§4.7), section labels with hairline rule (§5.1).
[List Bullet] [ ] Implement furigana via <ruby> everywhere.
[List Bullet] [ ] Audit: no emoji, no shadows, no gradients, no decorative icons in body text.
[List Bullet] [ ] Audit: no font weight 600 or 700 anywhere.
[List Bullet] [ ] Test in light and dark mode at every page.
[List Bullet] [ ] Test on mobile 375px, tablet 768px, desktop 1280px.
[List Bullet] [ ] Run Lighthouse: Accessibility >= 95.

[Heading 2] 13. What this design is and is not
It is:
[List Bullet] Quiet, slow, considered.
[List Bullet] Built to look right after 100 hours of use, not in the App Store screenshot.
[List Bullet] Heavily reliant on disciplined typography and whitespace.
[List Bullet] A reflection of a real Japanese aesthetic tradition (Muji, MoMA, Kinokuniya).
It is not:
[List Bullet] Friendly, warm, gamified, or "fun."
[List Bullet] Designed for users who want streaks, badges, XP, or push notifications.
[List Bullet] Forgiving of half-hearted execution. A 70% implementation will look worse than the current bland design. Either commit fully or pick a more forgiving theme.
If you can't ship it at 95% fidelity to this spec, ship something more forgiving. This design works because of its discipline. Halfway is worse than starting over.

*End of design system.*

[Heading 1] Chapter 2. UI Testing Plan
Source: feedback/ui-testing-plan.md

[Heading 1] JLPT N5 Tutor - UI-Level Testing Plan
Site under test: https://gauravaccentureproducts.github.io/jlpt-n5-tutor/
Local: index.html served from project root (any static file server).
Last updated: 2026-04-30 (synced to UX Brief 2 Phases 1-4 + Learn hub + per-vocab detail; SW jlpt-n5-tutor-v18)
Scope: End-to-end UI testing of the deployed web app from every reasonable perspective. Engine-layer unit tests (currently 37/37 in tests.html) are out of scope here - this document covers what those tests cannot reach.
> ★ Foundational principle: This app's purpose is to teach correct Japanese. Every other concern in this plan (performance, accessibility, design, even cross-browser support) is subordinate. §12 - Japanese language accuracy & content integrity is the bar this app must clear; it is enforced as a hard CI release blocker (§12.1), spot-checked at every release (§12.2), and re-audited quarterly (§12.3). Read §12 first. Everything else follows.

[Heading 2] 0. How to use this plan
Each section below names a perspective, the questions that perspective asks, the specific tests to run, and (where useful) the tool that automates it.
The plan is intentionally large because the app has 17 routes × multiple sub-paths × 5 locales × multiple input modalities. Triage by:
[List Number] P0 - Smoke (5 min, every release): §17.1
[List Number] P1 - Pre-release gate (60 min, every release): §17.2 (extends existing gate in TASKS.md)
[List Number] P2 - Full regression (one full session, every milestone): §17.3
[List Number] P3 - Deep-dive perspectives (ad-hoc / quarterly): §1-§16
Treat §1-§16 as a catalog of perspectives. Pick the ones that match the change you just shipped.
[Heading 3] 0.1 Route map (current)


[Heading 2] 1. End-learner perspective (the target user)
The whole app exists for this person. Every other perspective is in service of it.
Persona: Adult self-studier, has 10-30 minutes a day, wants to pass N5. Has tried Duolingo / Anki / Memrise / Bunpro and is skeptical.
[Heading 3] 1.1 The "first lesson" journey

Acceptance: A new user can complete a lesson without reading any documentation.
[Heading 3] 1.2 The "did I really learn it" journey

Acceptance: SRS schedule matches SM-2 algorithm (verified in tests.html); UI surfaces what's due, what's overdue, and what's mastered.
[Heading 3] 1.3 Test-mode honesty

Acceptance: The user can take 5 mock tests and the experience feels different each time.

[Heading 2] 2. First-time-visitor perspective
Question: "What is this site? Should I trust it? How do I start?"

Tool: Run a Lighthouse "Best Practices" audit; manually time-to-interactive on Slow-4G in DevTools.

[Heading 2] 3. Returning-visitor perspective
Question: "Where was I? What's due today?"

Acceptance: A returning user knows exactly what to click within 3 seconds.

[Heading 2] 4. Mobile-user perspective (≤480px)
Question: "Can I study on the train?"

Tool: Chrome DevTools device emulation, real iPhone + Pixel via BrowserStack.

[Heading 2] 5. Accessibility perspective (WCAG 2.1 AA)
Question: "Can a screen-reader user pass N5 with this?"
[Heading 3] 5.1 Automated checks

Target: zero serious or critical axe violations on every route.
[Heading 3] 5.2 Manual checks (axe cannot test)

[Heading 3] 5.3 Cognitive accessibility


[Heading 2] 6. Internationalization perspective (5 locales)
Locales: en, vi (Vietnamese), id (Indonesian), ne (Nepali), zh (Chinese)
[Heading 3] 6.1 Per-locale checks
For each locale, switch to it via Settings and run §17.1 smoke. Specifically check:

[Heading 3] 6.2 Cross-locale data integrity

Tool: Diff locales/en.json against the others - report missing keys.

[Heading 2] 7. Slow-connection perspective (Slow-4G / 3G)
Question: "Does this load on a metro / village WiFi?"

Tool: DevTools Network throttle = Slow 4G; Lighthouse mobile profile.

[Heading 2] 8. Offline perspective
Question: "Can I study on a flight?"

Tool: DevTools "Offline" checkbox; Application > Service Workers panel.

[Heading 2] 9. Power-user perspective
Question: "Can I move at the speed of thought?"

Tool: Manual; record a 60s screencast of "I want to drill kosoado for 5 mins" and count clicks.

[Heading 2] 10. Cross-browser perspective

Per-browser smoke: §17.1 plus three browser-specific traps:
[List Bullet] Safari: audio playback (especially first-tap requirement on iOS), <ruby> fallback, IndexedDB quirks
[List Bullet] Firefox: font fallback chain (Noto Sans JP), ServiceWorker scope
[List Bullet] Edge: legacy compat headers (HSTS / referrer policy)
Tool: BrowserStack Live or Sauce Labs for the long tail.

[Heading 2] 11. Cross-OS perspective

Tool: Real-device verification via BrowserStack or local VMs.

[Heading 2] 12. Japanese language accuracy & content integrity (MOST IMPORTANT)
> Foundational principle: Every other perspective in this plan assumes the Japanese itself is correct. A perfectly accessible, fast-loading, beautifully-designed app that teaches wrong Japanese is actively harmful. JA accuracy is the bar this app is built to clear. It outranks performance, design, and even accessibility.
>
> The KB content has been audited 9 times (see verification.md Passes 1-9). This section's job is to prevent regressions between audits and to keep the audit's invariants enforceable in CI.
This section has three layers:
[List Bullet] §12.1 Automated content invariants — the audit's checks running on every PR
[List Bullet] §12.2 Runtime JA accuracy spot-checks — what to verify in the live app
[List Bullet] §12.3 Periodic re-audit cadence — Pass-N protocol
[Heading 3] 12.1 Automated content invariants in CI
Re-encode the Pass-9 cross-file consistency checks (verification.md §8.6) as automated assertions. Any future JSON / KB edit that breaks one fails the build.

Beyond X-6.1–X-6.7, also enforce:

Tool: A tools/check_content_integrity.py script (or .js) wired into the CI workflow from §19. Failures block merge.
# Skeleton (place in tools/check_content_integrity.py)
INVARIANTS = [
    check_x_6_1_kanji_catalog,
    check_x_6_2_reading_consistency,
    check_x_6_3_no_mixed_kanji_kana,
    # ... etc
    check_ja_1_stem_kanji_scope,
    # ... etc
    check_ja_8_q_count_integrity,
]
failures = [c.__name__ for c in INVARIANTS if not c()]
sys.exit(1 if failures else 0)

Each check is one short function. Total: ~16 invariants, ~150 lines of Python.
[Heading 3] 12.2 Runtime JA accuracy spot-checks (during UI testing)
The CI invariants catch structural breakage in the JSON. They cannot catch semantic breakage — for example, a perfectly-formed question whose Japanese is unnatural. That requires human review.
For every UI test session (P0 / P1 / P2), include the following JA spot-checks:
12.2.1 Always check (P0 smoke, 30 seconds)
[List Bullet] [ ] Pick any 3 random questions from Test mode. Read the stem and answer aloud as a Japanese speaker would. Sounds natural? If anything jars, log to a new audit-pass entry.
[List Bullet] [ ] Open a Learn lesson in vi locale. Confirm the Japanese stays Japanese (UI chrome translates; example sentences and furigana do not).
[List Bullet] [ ] Trigger a kanji popover (e.g., on 学). Verify on/kun readings match kanji_n5.md.
12.2.2 Per-route check (P1 gate, 5 minutes)
For each of the 17 routes that surfaces JA content:

Pass condition: every spot-check succeeds, OR the failure is logged as a new finding and triaged.
12.2.3 Cross-perspective JA checks
[List Bullet] JA × i18n (§6): Switching locales must not corrupt Japanese text rendering. Specifically, no half-width katakana when full-width was authored; no kanji turned into "?" boxes.
[List Bullet] JA × a11y (§5): Screen readers should announce furigana correctly (or aria-hidden it if double-announcement happens). NVDA + Japanese voice should pronounce 学校 as がっこう, not as がく-こう.
[List Bullet] JA × audio (§7-§8): TTS audio should match the on-screen reading. If 今日 is shown with reading きょう, the audio should say きょう, not こんにち. Specific regression guard (Pass-10): numbers must read in Japanese, not English. Every audio-bearing example with a digit (e.g. 本を 3さつ 読みました) must read "さんさつ" via the normalize_for_tts() digit→kanji substitution; "スリーさつ" is a hard fail. Spot-check by playing one MP3 from each of audio/grammar/n5-005.0.mp3 (7じ), audio/listening/n5.listen.001.mp3 (3時), audio/reading/n5.read.002.mp3 (6時, 9時, 5時, 10時) — every digit should be rendered in Japanese.
[List Bullet] JA × visual (§16): Furigana ruby alignment is correct (centered above base text); no overlapping; readable at 100% zoom and at 200% zoom.
[Heading 3] 12.3 Periodic re-audit (Pass-N protocol)
Cadence: Every quarter, OR after any batch of 10+ KB edits, OR before any major version bump.
Process:
[List Number] Pick an audit lens for this pass: paper-maker (Pass 6), native-teacher (Pass 8), external-brief (Pass 9), or new (e.g., "child-learner readability"). Each lens surfaces different findings.
[List Number] Read all 5 question files + 4 catalog files end-to-end.
[List Number] Log findings with severity (CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW).
[List Number] Register in TASKS.md as "Pass N+1 - <lens name>".
[List Number] Fix in priority order. Each fix → update verification.md immediately.
[List Number] Re-run §12.1 invariants. All must pass.
[List Number] Mark Pass N+1 closed in verification.md. Update cumulative tally.
Why this matters: Static checks (§12.1) catch structural regressions but cannot catch *new* unnatural-Japanese introduced by an edit. Only a human re-read finds those. The Pass-N protocol ensures the bar set by Passes 1-9 stays raised over time.
[Heading 3] 12.4 Content-integrity (UI ↔ data mapping)
These were the original §12 checks — kept here because they're still important, but subordinate to JA accuracy.

Tool: Same tools/check_content_integrity.py from §12.1; these are added as additional invariants (UI-1 through UI-9).
[Heading 3] 12.5 Severity escalation
If a §12.1 invariant fires in CI, treat it as a release blocker. JA accuracy regressions cannot ship.
If a §12.2 spot-check finds a semantic issue mid-test:
[List Bullet] CRITICAL (factual error, internal contradiction, would teach wrong Japanese) → block the release; open a hotfix Pass-N entry; fix immediately
[List Bullet] HIGH (unidiomatic, register clash, or policy violation) → log; fix in next milestone
[List Bullet] MEDIUM / LOW → log; batch into next quarterly Pass-N audit

Tool: Lighthouse CI in GitHub Actions; failing budget should fail the build.

This is a static no-login no-server app, but still:

Tool: Mozilla Observatory for headers; manual DevTools network audit.

Tool: Chrome DevTools > Application > Manifest; PWA Builder.

Tool: Playwright toHaveScreenshot(); or Percy/Chromatic SaaS.

[Heading 2] 17. Test execution levels
[Heading 3] 17.1 P0 - Smoke (5 minutes, every release)
Pre-flight before any deploy:
[List Bullet] [ ] §12.1 invariants pass (all 18 content checks green - this is a HARD release blocker; X-6.8 digit-in-TTS and X-6.9 primary-reading sanity are part of this set)
[List Bullet] [ ] §12.2.1 JA spot-check (3 random questions read naturally; kanji popover matches catalog)
[List Bullet] [ ] Audio digit spot-check (Pass-10 regression): play any MP3 from audio/grammar/n5-005.0.mp3 (7じに) and audio/listening/n5.listen.003.mp3 (8時半 / 9時). Numbers must read in Japanese (しちじ / はちじはん / くじ), never in English ("seven" / "eight" / "nine" / "スリー").
[List Bullet] [ ] Home page loads. Title is correct. No console errors. Trust strip visible above the fold.
[List Bullet] [ ] Click "Learn" → 5-card hub shows (Grammar / Vocabulary / Kanji / Dokkai / Listening).
[List Bullet] [ ] Click "Grammar" card → grammar TOC of cards loads; click any card → pattern detail shows pattern + EN + JA meaning + ≥1 example with translation.
[List Bullet] [ ] Click "Vocabulary" card → vocab cards load; click any card → vocab detail shows form / reading / gloss / examples.
[List Bullet] [ ] Click "Kanji" card → kanji card grid loads; click any card → kanji detail shows on/kun/meaning.
[List Bullet] [ ] Click "Test" → question shows. Pick an answer → moves to next.
[List Bullet] [ ] Click "Settings" → switch locale to vi → UI updates. Switch furigana to "Always show" → ruby renders on every kanji. Switch back to "Hide on known kanji" → previously-marked kanji stay un-rubied.
[List Bullet] [ ] Open kanji popover (click any glyph) → toggle "I know this kanji" → reload → ruby still hidden on that kanji.
[List Bullet] [ ] Refresh page. Locale + furigana mode + "I know this" flags + streak all persist.
[List Bullet] [ ] Open DevTools → Network panel → no third-party requests.
[List Bullet] [ ] Lighthouse mobile run completes; score not regressed by >5pts.
[Heading 3] 17.2 P1 - Pre-release gate (60 minutes, every release)
Already covered by §9 of original Brief 1; extend with:
[List Bullet] [ ] §12.1 invariants pass (re-confirmed after any KB / data edit)
[List Bullet] [ ] §12.2.2 per-route JA spot-check (every route surfacing JA content is sampled; semantic issues logged)
[List Bullet] [ ] All 17 routes + sub-paths load without console errors (use for route in routes; navigate; assert no error). Sub-paths to include: #/learn/grammar, #/learn/vocab, #/learn/vocab/<form>, #/learn/<patternId>, #/kanji/<glyph>, #/test/<n>.
[List Bullet] [ ] axe-core shows zero serious violations on Home, Learn (hub + grammar TOC + vocab list + vocab detail), Test, Review, Settings, Kanji index, Kanji detail.
[List Bullet] [ ] Lighthouse mobile Perf/A11y/PWA/SEO all ≥90.
[List Bullet] [ ] Offline mode: kill network, reload home, app still works. Verify SW served stale shell + content.
[List Bullet] [ ] Export progress JSON (schemaVersion 2). Wipe localStorage with typed-phrase reset. Import. All 5 storage keys restored (settings, history, results, knownKanji, streak).
[List Bullet] [ ] PWA install prompt fires once on supported browser; "Not now" persists; doesn't re-prompt.
[List Bullet] [ ] SW update toast: bump cache version locally, reload, expect "Update available" toast → click Reload → new shell active.
[List Bullet] [ ] Run full tests.html; 37/37 pass.
[Heading 3] 17.3 P2 - Full regression (one full session, every milestone)
Run §1-§16 systematically. Time budget ~4 hours for a single tester.
Mandatory in P2:
[List Bullet] [ ] §12.3 Pass-N re-audit of all 5 question files + 4 catalog files from one chosen lens (paper-maker / native-teacher / external-brief / new lens). Document findings as a new Pass-N entry in verification.md.
Document findings in a Pass-N audit entry.
[Heading 3] 17.4 P3 - Deep-dive perspectives (ad-hoc)
When a specific change ships (e.g., "we redesigned the Review screen"), pick the perspective most affected and run that section deeply. Don't run the whole §1-§16 every time.
Special case: Any change touching KnowledgeBank/*.md or data/*.json automatically triggers §12.1 (CI invariants) AND §12.2.1 (smoke JA spot-check) AND a focused §12.2.2 on the affected route. JA accuracy is non-negotiable.

[Heading 2] 18. Recommended tooling stack
For a static no-server JLPT app maintained by 1-2 people:

Anti-pattern: Don't add Cypress / Selenium / Puppeteer alongside Playwright. Pick one.

[Heading 2] 19. CI integration
Add a single GitHub Actions workflow that runs on every PR to main:
name: ui-regression
on:
  pull_request:
    branches: [main]
jobs:
  test:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v4
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npx playwright install --with-deps
      - run: npx playwright test
      - run: npx @lhci/cli autorun
      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        if: always()
        with:
          name: test-artifacts
          path: |
            playwright-report/
            .lighthouseci/

Fail the build on:
[List Bullet] Any Playwright test failure
[List Bullet] axe serious or critical violation
[List Bullet] Lighthouse Perf/A11y drop >5pts vs baseline
[List Bullet] Visual regression > 0.1% pixel diff (after a manual approval workflow)

[Heading 2] 20. Heuristic checklist (Nielsen 10, applied to this app)
For ad-hoc UX review:
[List Number] Visibility of system status - Loading skeletons, save indicators, streak progress all visible.
[List Number] Match between system and real world - "Daily Drill" not "Spaced repetition queue v2."
[List Number] User control & freedom - Can pause / quit / undo any action without penalty.
[List Number] Consistency & standards - All "next" buttons same shape/place; all destructive actions confirm.
[List Number] Error prevention - Disabled states for invalid actions; confirm before wiping progress.
[List Number] Recognition over recall - Show furigana toggle state; show selected option visually, not just by tab focus.
[List Number] Flexibility & efficiency - Keyboard shortcuts for power users; touch-friendly for mobile.
[List Number] Aesthetic & minimalist design - Don't add decoration that doesn't aid learning.
[List Number] Help users recognize & recover from errors - "You picked X, the answer was Y because Z."
[List Number] Help & documentation - ? key for shortcut cheatsheet; per-screen help link if needed.

[Heading 2] 21. What this plan deliberately does NOT cover
[List Bullet] Backend testing - there is no backend.
[List Bullet] Auth / accounts - not in scope; PWA design is no-login (Brief 1 hard constraint #3).
[List Bullet] Cloud sync - out of scope (Brief 1 #2 / Out-of-scope).
[List Bullet] Speaking practice - out of scope (microphone input).
[List Bullet] Engine math (SRS intervals) - already covered by tests.html.
Note on JA correctness: Earlier drafts of this plan listed "Content correctness in JA" as out of scope. That was wrong. JA accuracy is the foundational concern of this app and is now §12 — the most important perspective in the plan. Audit Passes 1-9 in verification.md set the bar; §12.1 keeps it enforced in CI; §12.2 keeps it spot-checked at runtime; §12.3 keeps it re-audited every quarter.

[Heading 2] 22. Acceptance criteria for this plan
The plan itself is "in use" when:
[List Number] §12.1 invariants are enforceable — tools/check_content_integrity.py exists, runs all 16 checks, and is wired into CI as a release blocker.
[List Number] §12.2 spot-checks are documented — every release log includes the 3-question JA spot-check from §12.2.1.
[List Number] §12.3 re-audit cadence is set — calendar reminder for quarterly Pass-N re-audit; or trigger on any 10+ KB-edit batch.
[List Number] Every release runs §17.1 (P0 smoke).
[List Number] Every release runs §17.2 (P1 gate); failures block deploy.
[List Number] Every milestone runs §17.3 (P2 full regression); findings logged as Pass-N audit entry.
[List Number] Tooling from §18 is installed in the repo with a package.json script entry point.
[List Number] CI from §19 is wired and green on main.
When all eight hold, the app has a defensible "we teach correct Japanese, and we test it" story.

[Heading 1] Chapter 3. Procedure Manual — Building the Next JLPT Level App
Source: <JLPT-root>/procedure-manual-build-next-jlpt-level.md (includes Appendix B + Appendix C + TASKS.md template inline)

[Heading 1] Procedure Manual — Building the Next JLPT Level App
Source project: JLPT N5 Tutor (this repo)
Target audience: any future JLPT level app (N4, N3, N2, N1) using the same architecture
Prepared: 2026-05-01 from accumulated N5 build experience (Phases 1-5 + Passes 1-19)
Status: Living document — update as each next-level build adds new lessons
This manual is written prescriptively. Where N5 hit a problem, the manual tells the next level how to avoid it. Generic best-practice advice has been omitted; only N5-specific learnings are included.
[Heading 2] How to read this manual: level placeholders
This manual is level-agnostic. It documents the procedure for building ANY next JLPT level app (N4, N3, N2, N1) starting from the N5 source. To keep the prose readable while remaining precise, the following conventions are used:
[List Bullet] `<L>` = the target level number you are building (4 for N4, 3 for N3, 2 for N2, 1 for N1).
[List Bullet] `<P>` = the prior level number you are building from (typically <L>+1; usually 5 if you start from this N5 repo, but can be any lower-numbered level once intermediate apps exist).
[List Bullet] `<L-1>` = one level above the target (e.g., when <L>=4, <L-1>=3; this is the level whose grammar starts to "leak in" as borderline content). JLPT levels are numbered with N1 as the highest, so smaller <L> is harder.
[List Bullet] `N<L>` in narrative = "the target level" (read as N4 / N3 / N2 / N1 depending on which build you are doing).
[List Bullet] `n<L>-` / `n<L>.` / `n<L>_` in code, paths, and IDs = the lowercase level prefix (read as n4-, n3-, etc.).
[List Bullet] "N5" appearances refer specifically to the source project (this repo) and remain literal regardless of which target level you are building. If you ever build a NEW source level lower than N5 (unlikely — N5 is the lowest JLPT level), substitute N<P> for "N5" throughout.
The §0 scope table below shows the actual size deltas at each level transition; everything else uses placeholders so the manual works whether you are building N4, N3, N2, or N1.
When the manual references "the source level" or "the source repo", it always means N5 (this repo) unless an explicit <P> token is used.

[Heading 2] 0.A One-instruction autonomous-build contract
The user has authorised the agent to execute the entire next-level build on a single instruction. This section defines exactly what that means, so the agent can act without asking for clarification.
[Heading 3] 0.A.1 Trigger phrases
If the user says any of the following (case-insensitive, paraphrasing OK), execute the full procedure in §A.12 autonomously:
[List Bullet] "build the next level" / "make the next level" / "go build the next level"
[List Bullet] "build N<L>" / "make N<L>" / "scaffold N<L>" / "start N<L>" — where <L> ∈ {4, 3, 2, 1}
[List Bullet] "go" — when the immediately-prior conversation context was about building the next level
[List Bullet] "ya" / "yes" / "proceed" — when responding to an offer of "shall I build N<L>?"
Don't pause for confirmation after any of these. Don't ask "are you sure?" / "which level?" / "where to put it?" — apply the defaults below.
[Heading 3] 0.A.2 Implicit-input defaults (no questions asked)
When the trigger fires, derive every required input from context with these defaults:

If ANY of the above defaults will produce something the user would reject, the agent's job is to log the assumption in TASKS.md Pass-1 so a human pass can revisit. Do not pause execution to ask.
[Heading 3] 0.A.3 Halt conditions (the only times the agent stops to ask)
The agent halts and asks ONLY if a default cannot be safely applied:
[List Number] Target directory non-empty. Refuse to overwrite. Halt: "Target <path> is non-empty. Clear it manually or specify a different path."
[List Number] No source repo found. Halt: "Cannot identify source level — no built JLPT repo found at sibling paths. Specify --source <path> or build N5 first."
[List Number] Unrecognised target level. If the user says e.g. "build N0" or "build N6" (outside JLPT spec). Halt: "Target level out of range — JLPT levels are N1 (highest) to N5 (lowest)."
[List Number] Network unavailable AND inventory file missing. If feedback/n<L>-*-inventory*.md doesn't exist in the source repo AND WebFetch fails on all the §A.7 source URLs. Halt: "Cannot resolve N<L> content inventories — neither local nor web sources reachable."
[List Number] Destructive operation requested by trigger. If the trigger phrase includes "delete", "wipe", "reset", "force-overwrite", etc. Always halt and confirm.
Outside of these five cases, do not halt. The agent must complete §A.12 end-to-end, committing partial state at each checkpoint so a future invocation can resume.
[Heading 3] 0.A.4 What the agent delivers on completion
A successful one-instruction build delivers, at minimum:
[List Number] A new git repo at <JLPT-root>/N<L>/ initialized with the directory structure of §1.1, all files present (skeleton content allowed per §A.4 layer 0-3).
[List Number] All §A.5 definition-of-done items GREEN for layers 0-2 (build pipeline, schemas, UI shell). Layers 3-7 (content) green AT LEAST to the §A.4 minimum-viable subset.
[List Number] A populated `TASKS.md` with: status snapshot, current-pass section, deferred items, EB backlog, and explicit "what was authored vs deferred" record.
[List Number] A populated `MEMORY.md` ≤200 lines summarising what the next session inherits.
[List Number] All initial commits pushed to a remote of the agent's choosing (default: a sibling org/user-page on the same git host as the source repo's origin).
[List Number] A summary report in chat (or stdout if non-interactive) following the §A.13 handoff format.
[Heading 3] 0.A.5 Skip-permissions posture
A one-instruction build assumes the agent runs in --dangerously-skip-permissions mode (or equivalent). The deny-list in .claude/settings.local.json (which the bootstrap copies from source) still gates destructive ops. The agent does NOT need to ask permission for normal git / file / fetch operations during the build.
If skip-permissions is not active and the agent's runtime keeps prompting, the build will stall. The agent should detect this on the second prompt and halt with: "Cannot run autonomously without --dangerously-skip-permissions. Restart with the flag and re-run the trigger."

[Heading 2] ⚠ Operating modes — read this first
This document supports two execution modes. The bulk of the manual (§§0–16) was written for Mode A. Appendix A (§17+) carries the supplements required for Mode B.
[Heading 3] Mode A — human team + N5 repo as co-resident reference (default)
[List Bullet] Reader: a human + AI assistant (Claude Code) working together over weeks-to-months.
[List Bullet] Required inputs: this manual and the N5 source repo at a known path (so "copy from N5" instructions resolve to actual files).
[List Bullet] The manual reads as a prescriptive playbook; "see N5", "copy from N5", "port verbatim" are concrete actions with files to read.
[List Bullet] Estimated effort: 17-25 weeks (per §13).
[Heading 3] Mode B — zero-interaction one-shot agent (limited)
[List Bullet] Reader: a single coding-agent run with no human in the loop.
[List Bullet] Required inputs: this manual plus the entire N5 repo as a tarball or directory that the agent can read.
[List Bullet] Without the N5 repo, this manual is approximately a table of contents — most "copy from N5" / "port" instructions are unresolvable, schemas and content inventories are not embedded, and one-shot completion is not feasible.
[List Bullet] Even WITH the N5 repo: a one-shot agent should use Appendix A — it provides default decisions for the §15 open questions, fallback procedures for external-blocked items, a definition-of-done, and a minimum-viable subset to ship if the full scope can't fit in one run.
[List Bullet] Honest expectation: a zero-interaction agent producing a *complete* N<L> app in one run is unrealistic. Realistic one-shot deliverable = scaffolded skeleton (build pipeline, schemas, CI, UI shell, ~20% of content) that a human team finishes in subsequent passes.
This split is a direct response to the Pass-20 manual review (feedback/procedure-manual-review-issues.md, 40 issues across 6 risk categories). The review's core finding stands: this manual is a *playbook*, not a *self-contained build spec*. Pass-20 closure ships in two parts:
[List Bullet] Appendix A (§17 of this file) — closes 15 issues by adding operating-modes preamble, default decisions, fallback procedures, MVS, definition of done, schemas recipe, source authorities, exam structure, SM-2 params, furigana procedure.
[List Bullet] Appendix B (separate file `procedure-manual-appendix-b-extracted-from-n5.md`) — closes the remaining "extract from N5" cluster (12 items) by directly extracting schemas, rules, and conventions from the N5 codebase. Sections B.1 through B.12 cover: vocab-ID slug rule (P0), audio manifest schema (P0), JSON schemas for all data files, i18n locale format, Playwright test framework, UI module list, KB markdown grammar (BNF), all 28 invariant rule specifications, Diagnostic Summary algorithm, kanji-tier interaction, external-corpus URL list per level, and content-inventory extraction recipes (N4 kanji/vocab/grammar via authoritative-source scripts, since the agent must FETCH not INVENT content).
[List Bullet] Appendix C (separate file `procedure-manual-appendix-c-pass22-polish.md`) — closes 7 of the 10 Pass-22 polish items (F-22.1 distractor rubric, F-22.2 ko-so-a-do scene-context formatting standard, F-22.3 JA-2/JA-23 invariant interaction clarification, F-22.4 augmented-set escape-valve guard via WHY-comment regex spec, F-22.6 auto-generation stop-condition formalization, F-22.8 full PWA spec, F-22.9 same-pattern-string conflict-resolution rule). The other two Pass-22 items live in their own files: F-22.5 LLM-audit prompt at tools/prompts/llm_audit.prompt.md, F-22.7 TASKS.md canonical template at specifications/tasks-md-template.md.
Together, Appendices A + B + C close 36 of 40 Pass-20 issues plus 9 of the 10 Pass-22 polish items. Remaining open: F-22.4 / F-22.5 code-side changes (the actual JA-25 invariant in tools/check_content_integrity.py and the SYSTEM_PROMPT extraction in tools/llm_audit.py) — deferred to a future commit because the parallel session was active on those tool files at this commit's time.

[Heading 2] 0. Scope of "next level"
The same playbook scales N5 → N4 → N3 → N2 → N1. Each level transition adds the deltas shown below. To use this manual, locate the row matching your target <L> and source <P> (typically <P>=<L>+1):

The N5 → N4 transition is the smallest content jump but introduces the most architectural decisions (tier taxonomy, kanji-policy contention, borderline-grammar handling). For N3 and lower (<L> ≤ 3) it is mostly content scaling once those one-time architectural choices are committed.

[Heading 2] 1. Day 0 — Repo bootstrap (1-2 hours)
[Heading 3] 1.1 Directory structure (copy from N5)
.
├── .claude/
│   ├── CLAUDE.md           # binding rule for Claude Code automation
│   └── settings.local.json # personal permission overrides (gitignored)
├── .github/
│   └── workflows/
│       └── content-integrity.yml
├── KnowledgeBank/          # source-of-truth Markdown
│   ├── grammar_n<L>.md
│   ├── kanji_n<L>.md
│   ├── vocabulary_n<L>.md
│   ├── sources.md
│   ├── moji_questions_n<L>.md
│   ├── goi_questions_n<L>.md
│   ├── bunpou_questions_n<L>.md
│   ├── dokkai_questions_n<L>.md
│   ├── chokai_questions_n<L>.md   # NEW: listening was inline at N5; promote to its own file at N<L> (any next level)
│   └── authentic_extracted_n<L>.md
